# STATE — read this first, then the graph

One page, kept current, so a new session spends zero tokens re-deriving where
the project stands. Deep history lives in `decisions.md` / `rejected.md` /
`measurements.md`; the index is `graph.json` (`node scripts/context.mjs`).
**If this file and any other disagree, the other files win — fix this one.**

Last updated: 2026-08-27, and this line is the ONLY one of its kind in this
file. **Do not add a "Last updated" line here.** Put what your session did in
the SESSION LOG at the bottom of this page instead.

Why that rule exists, so it is not undone as fussiness: this header is not
append-only, but the merge helper that resolves `context/` conflicts unions
append-only files line by line. Every agent that added its own header line here
therefore survived the union, and eight of them stacked up twice. The bottom of
the file IS append-only, so a line placed there merges correctly by
construction rather than by anyone remembering to tidy up.

**Other agents:** `AGENTS.md` at the repo root is the tool-neutral entry point
and points back here. `CLAUDE.md` carries the same rules for Claude Code.

## What the product is

Vyakti: a self-serve platform where ANYONE builds an exact AI version of
themselves — mind, voice, relation, long-term continuity — from their own
context (files, links, channels, calls), iterates on it frictionlessly, and
deploys it anywhere. It **remembers each person it talks to** and carries a
**measured guarantee it still sounds like them**. Edtech (JEE teachers →
student app) is the first vertical and wedge, not the boundary
(`horizontal-platform-reweight`, 2026-08-26). North star and binding consequences:
`docs/gurukul/SPEC-GURUKUL.md` §8 (owner reweight, 2026-08-26).

Standing owner directives: in-house replica stack (self-hosted open weights
primary, vendors optional); Maya/Meera deprioritized as a product (its engine
gates stay); Fable runs the main loop, Opus 5 / Sonnet 5 run subagents.

## Where the code is

- Branch `claude/gurukul-platform`, PR #5 (draft, CI green).
- The union of the companion line (RelationalOS engine) and the voice-cloning
  line (Replica Lab studio). `docs/gurukul/SPEC-GURUKUL.md` is the map;
  `docs/gurukul/ROADMAP-100X.md` is the build order;
  `docs/gurukul/research/` holds the competitor / memory-science / voice-stack
  sweeps behind those decisions.
- Gates: `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` is **16 checks** as of 2026-08-27
  (14 without `NEON_URL`, which skips the two relational db gates). The count
  has grown as gates were added: WS-AM's layout readability, WS-AR's enrollment
  sample-rate mirror, WS-AS's enrollment bandwidth. `relcheck` is a hard gate
  wherever a URL is reachable. `npx vite build` alone is NOT a gate: it exits 0
  with type errors.

## START HERE: WHERE THIS ACTUALLY STANDS (2026-08-27 03:45Z)

Everything below this block is older and some of it is superseded. Read this
first; where it disagrees with anything further down, this wins.

**THE ONE OPEN PROBLEM: the clone does not sound like the owner.** Their
verdict, verbatim: "not even 0.05% similar", and the base voice is "very
western and not indian". Everything else in this product now works; this does
not, and it IS the product.

What is settled about it, measured rather than argued:
- **Cloning IS happening.** Three different enrollment references, same replica,
  text and seed, produced three different outputs (`b4ff277d88`, `65219c4f38`,
  `c7ba0591f8`). A model ignoring its reference is byte identical across arms.
  So do not go looking for a disconnected wire; the fault was reference QUALITY.
- **The reference was 8 kHz audio wearing a 24 kHz label.** Measured by FFT:
  0.000458% of energy at or above 8 kHz. Two causes, and the second one is the
  one three sessions walked past: `separate` runs `sepformer-whamr16k` at 16 kHz
  UNCONDITIONALLY, including on a recording with one speaker and nothing to
  separate; and the 16 kHz bytes cut for window SCORING were being reused as the
  DELIVERED reference bytes, so skipping separation alone would not have helped.
- Both are fixed in `api/` and merged. After the fix, 0.0224% (about 49x).

**What is now done, and what remains:**
1. **The 24 kHz reference fix is live.** The Container Apps Job was rebuilt,
   pinned by digest and read back from Azure. The owner's real pipeline is at
   one ready source, eight complete jobs and VoiceGenome v2 draft. A fresh
   same-text Hindi preview from v2 returned 263,084 bytes of 24 kHz mono WAV,
   with PerTh verified. This proves the deployed v2 path runs; it does not
   prove acceptable likeness.
2. **There is still no authoritative speaker-similarity verdict for v2.**
   Nobody has measured likeness, only bandwidth, and bandwidth is not likeness.
   `vyakti-voice-evidence` has `ingress.external=false` so a session cannot
   reach it. The repo's known ceiling is 0.8869. **Do not report a proxy metric
   as fidelity.** An invented number here is the worst possible outcome.
3. DeepFilterNet3 on versus off is unmeasured. The owner's audio has real
   background noise, so it is a genuine trade, not an obvious call.
4. Browser upload is resumable and multi-file, but the account-level Supabase
   global Storage limit still refuses a 1 GiB bucket setting. Do not advertise
   1 GiB as live until that account setting is raised and a real >50 MiB TUS
   upload is completed.
5. **Vyakti Rooms v1 landed on top of all of the above, and does not change
   any of it (2026-09-03, WS-R1..R10).** The voice problem above is
   completely unchanged — **no human has listened** through a real earbench
   run, still (`no-human-has-listened`). What Rooms adds: an **identity path
   now exists behind a flag** (`VOICE_IDENTITY_CHALLENGE` +
   `VITE_VOICE_IDENTITY_CHALLENGE`, both default off, `docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md`
   §25) as an alternative to the still-undeployed Azure identity/liveness
   stack — it has never been exercised end to end and has no different-speaker
   control, so its 0.70 reject floor is inherited, not earned. The **follower
   Room now exists** at `/r/<slug>` (migration 071, applied live) with a leak
   battery proving its three scopes hold offline, but no real Room has ever
   been published or joined outside a fake `db` — see the LIVE table above.
   **Model keys are still absent** on the studio Vercel project — not new,
   not fixed by Rooms, and it means every LLM-backed reply on that project,
   Room included, cannot produce a completion regardless of how much of the
   above is wired correctly (`docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md` §25).

**Two live operational facts a new agent will otherwise rediscover painfully:**
- `vyakti-open-voice` (the GPU) has `minReplicas=0`. A warm GPU answers in about
  54 s; from cold it has exceeded 200 s and returned `wake_in_flight` ten polls
  running. The studio panel gives up at 180 s, which EQUALS the top of its own
  advertised "2 to 3 minutes". Same defect shape as the 60 s signature meeting a
  100-160 s cold start. Keeping a GPU warm bills continuously and is the owner's
  money: do not set `minReplicas` without being asked.
- The preview ledger (`neon-ledger.js`) structurally cannot open for ANY preview
  generation without an active runtime capability, because a preview's
  `voice_profile_id` is always NULL while the capability table's matching column
  is NOT NULL. Unrelated to reference quality. See
  `rejected.md#preview-ledger-requires-activation-this-replica-does-not-have`.

**What DOES work now, verified, so you do not re-audit it:** journey 15/15;
16/16 gates; CI green on PR #5; all eight DAG steps complete on the owner's real
822.7 s upload; voice genome v1 draft exists; `REPLICA_SELF_TEST_MODE=true` is

job; the five audio-protection vars are in Vercel; a real preview returns real

**The real cause of `separate`'s failure was confirmed structurally, not from a traceback** -- `services/voice-evidence/app.py`'s `/v1/analyze` ends in a bare `except Exception` with NO logging, so nothing about the failure ever reached Log Analytics across all 5 production attempts. What WAS confirmed: every OTHER exception path in that file raises a NAMED error (so every named validation, including the duration cap, provably passed); the replica's `restartCount` stayed 0 across all 5 attempts (rules out a container-level OOM kill, consistent with an in-process, catchable `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError`); and the only GPU-bound call in `_separate()` is one unchunked forward pass over the whole 822.72 s / 13.16M-sample waveform. Full reasoning: `context/measurements.md#separate-underlying-error-confirmed-structurally`.

A 30-minute lecture would still have failed here even with a raised cap: 1200 s
was a hard ceiling compiled into `app.py`, and raising it only moved the wall
from `diarize` to `separate`. `best-window-not-first-window` was the decided
fix and it is now LIVE for `separate`: never send a whole recording to the
embedder, send the best-scoring ~10 s window drawn from the owner's own
diarized cluster (never a second speaker's). WS-U measured window choice
moving fidelity more than fine-tuning does (0.7433-0.8058 spread versus a
0.0206 fine-tune delta), so windowing is both correct and higher quality.

### "Preview my voice" was broken in FIVE stacked layers

Each hid the next; all five are worth knowing because the pattern recurs.
1. The panel sends no `style`; the validator refused an ABSENT style with the
   same 400 as a typo, so every preview from the default path failed.
2. The route flattened that named 400 into an opaque 500 **and logged nothing**,
   which is why nobody could see layer 1.
3. The route only mapped errors carrying a `code`; sixteen validators in `api/`
   throw a bare `{status:400}`. Fixed as a class, not an instance.
4. **The audio-protection service was never deployed.** Every clip must
   carry the spoken disclosure and the PerTh watermark before delivery, so NO
   replica audio can leave for anyone.
   **Never stub or bypass this to get a green screen.**
5. **Root, found by WS-AR after 1-4 and the env pastes were all done and the
   owner still got `wav format unsupported` after a ten minute wait:** the
   enrollment reference the enhance stage PRODUCES and the rate
   `probeEnrollmentWav` DEMANDS before synthesis disagreed — 48 kHz emitted,
   24 kHz required, no test ever exercised both sides of that boundary
   together. 23 real dated `vy_replica_generation` rows on the owner's own
   replica prove this failed in production, repeatedly, before the fix.

Layers 1 to 3 are fixed in code. Layer 4 is fixed in infrastructure (WS-AL
deployed `vyakti-audio-protection`) and the five Vercel env vars are pasted
(session log, 22:20Z). Layer 5 is fixed in `services/voice-evidence/app.py`
(`enrollment-artifact-resamples-to-24k-inside-enhance`) and **this is the
layer that made the owner's most recent report** — the first four were
already closed by the time they hit it. **All five layers are now proven
closed together, on the owner's own replica, with real audio bytes coming
back** (`measurements.md#wav-format-unsupported-fixed-and-proven-end-to-end`).
A cross-boundary gate (`scripts/check-enrollment-sample-rate.mjs`) now asserts
the rate that produced layer 5 can never silently drift from the rate that
gates it again.

The lesson, now encoded: a refusal that chose its own 4xx keeps it, a
configuration absence answers 503 BY SHAPE rather than by a list of names, the
code is always logged, and a value emitted by one service and demanded by
another needs an assertion that they agree — because "the format gate exists"
and "the two sides of it agree" are different claims, and only testing the
whole path together catches when they do not.

### YouTube: measured to the end, and it needs a credential

- Audio extraction from a datacenter IP is **blocked** and every free lever is
  exhausted (all ten yt-dlp player clients; the PO-token provider moved the
  metadata check 5/6 versus 1/6 but yielded 0/12 audio bytes and the benefit
  vanished after ~40 requests).
- The transcript half does NOT rescue it. The sanctioned Data API answers a
  datacenter IP normally, but `captions.download` returns only manually
  uploaded tracks, which lecture channels do not have. Every auto-caption route
  goes through the same blocked player surface. There is no free transcript
  route hiding behind the audio problem.
- Recommendation on the table for the owner, **not purchased**: IPRoyal
  residential pay-as-you-go, about $0.077 per 15-minute lecture, $7 minimum,
  traffic does not expire. Switching it on is one env var
  (`MEDIA_EXTRACT_ROUTE=proxy` plus the URL); absent credentials refuse by name
  rather than falling back silently.

## The three ingestion/voice pipelines — status after the first real clone (2026-08-26)

**A real human voice has now been through the pipeline end to end.** The owner
supplied a 71 s consented Hinglish voice note; every stage below is a live
service response, not a claim.

| pipeline | status | evidence |
|---|---|---|
| **Voice cloning** | **WORKS, measured** | Zero-shot clone of the owner from their own 71 s reference. **ECAPA fidelity 0.7753** (p10 0.7479) against a **0.8869 self-vs-self ceiling**; verdict `warn`, so the activation gate correctly refuses (12 blockers). rtf 0.79–0.80 warm. n=2, spread 1e-6. **Zero-shot floor — NOT a claim about how it sounds** (that needs the blind ABX bench). |
| **Reference-window choice** | **measured, and it is the bigger lever** | Chatterbox truncates a reference to its **first 10 s** (s3gen) / **6 s** (T3 prompt); only the speaker embedding sees the rest. Which 10 s you pass spans **0.7433–0.8058** on the owner's voice — **0.0625, three times the fine-tune delta, at zero training cost** — and the best window beats every fine-tuned arm. Zero-shot; interaction with the adapter unmeasured. `reference-window-beats-the-finetune`. |
| **Per-speaker fine-tuning** | **BUILT and measured** | `services/voice-finetune` trains a LoRA adapter on a GPU job; the runtime loads it per request inside the same HMAC, disclosure and watermark path. On the owner's 71 s (62.1 s transcribed): **0.7753 → 0.7959, +0.0206, 18.4% of the gap to the ceiling, `warn` → `pass`**, n=2 spread 1e-5. Costs ~26% of synthesis speed (rtf 0.79 → 0.99). **A 71 s smoke test against a ≥30 min recommendation** — `lora-vs-zero-shot-71s`. |
| **voice-evidence round trip** | **WORKS — first ever run** | 71 s → 4 windows → 8 embeddings in **4 977 ms** warm; **176 s** cold start from zero. |
| **ASR (Sarvam)** | **WORKS** | sync `saarika:v2.5` 25 s → 200 in 4 134 ms (**hard 30 s cap**); batch `saaras:v3` 71 s → **5 diarized turns in 137 s**. `saarika:v2` is deprecated. |
| **transcript → sheet draft** | **WORKS** | 5 turns, 127 tokens, **92 honest `gaps`**, 8 phrase candidates. |
| **upload → finalize** | **finalize was BROKEN; fixed in tree, deployment behind it** | `replicaObjectInfo` parsed a HEAD-style route as JSON, so EVERY finalize failed closed and **nothing downstream of storage had ever executed for anyone**. One redeploy away. |
| **YouTube extraction** | **RUN, and the answer is split** | `services/media-extract` is DEPLOYED (`vyakti-media-extract`, Azure Container Apps, CPU, scale-to-zero, yt-dlp 2026.08.19, `/healthz` 200 in 47.9 s cold). Against real YouTube from that egress: **`/v1/enumerate` WORKS** (200 in 13.9 s, real ids — first ever live run of the channel lane) and **`/v1/extract` is BLOCKED** — `extractor_bot_check` in 2–3 s at the metadata probe, on **all 10** player clients yt-dlp offers. Lever 1 is exhausted; levers 2 (cookies) and 3 (proxy) need credentials nobody has yet and were NOT guessed at. `youtube-extraction-blocked-from-azure`. |
| **One link → one clone** (WS-AD) | code-complete, gated offline, **extraction step blocked upstream** | `/api/video-enroll`, `api/_video-enroll{.js,/windows.js,/quota.js}`, migration **060**, `src/studio/VideoEnrollPanel.tsx`. Paste one video URL → attest the channel (WS-S's table, reused) → extract → **score every ~10 s window and condition on the best one anywhere in the video** → ASR → sheet draft. `evals/videoenroll.mjs`, 80 checks, wired into `evals/run.mjs`. Migration 060 UNAPPLIED; `promoteReference` is a declared seam this deploy does not supply, so `reference_promoted` is false and says so. |

**Known-open, deliberately not guessed at:**
- Code-switch ratio reads 0.000 on visibly bilingual speech — `HINDI_MARKER_WORDS` is romanised, Sarvam returns Devanagari. Needs a decision (transliterate / extend lexicon / different model), not a patch.
- Fidelity cannot be persisted or clear activation until a voice profile + biometric consent + human liveness challenge exist.
- HMAC skew window (60 s) is shorter than `voice-evidence`'s 176 s cold start, so the waking request returns 401 — an auth error for a latency problem. Worked around by pinging `/healthz` first. **The open-voice lane fails differently and this line used to blur the two:** its broker verifies the signature the moment the request lands and only then forwards it, so a cold *GPU runtime* is a **timeout**, not a 401. Owned as of WS-W by `api/_voice/warmup.js` for the studio panel. **WS-AK has now measured the processing worker's copy of it on production and it is exactly this:** the deployed job's first `diarize` against a cold replica returned 401 `transport_signature_invalid`, the same code from a warm replica authenticated in 20 s, and the two HMAC secrets were confirmed identical by digest first so the obvious wrong fix (rotate the key) was not taken. Still unowned, now with numbers (`measurements.md#voice-evidence-round-trip-first-ever`, `rejected.md#signing-before-a-cold-start-cannot-authenticate`).

**The command, once env is set:** `node scripts/first-clone.mjs /path/to/voice.wav "Name"`
(input must be 24 kHz mono PCM16: `ffmpeg -i in.m4a -ac 1 -ar 24000 -c:a pcm_s16le out.wav`).

## What is NOT live

- **Voice QUALITY**: a consented reference HAS now been cloned and scored

  bench has run**, and nothing may be claimed about how a clone sounds until
  the bench in `docs/gurukul/research/voice-stack.md` does. The INSTRUMENT for
  that bench now exists and is verified mechanically (WS-V,
  `earbench-is-the-listening-instrument`, run instructions in
  `docs/gurukul/EARBENCH.md`, one command: `node scripts/earbench.mjs stimuli`
  → `listen` → `score`). **No human has listened through it**
  (`no-human-has-listened`), and its two network reads — the Supabase reference
  and the Azure synthesis — have never run for want of credentials. Note the
  defect it surfaced: every synthesised clip SPEAKS the disclosure sentence and
  so unblinds any listening test unless trimmed
  (`disclosure-announces-the-clone`). Cold start (161 s
  ready, 504 at 242 s for the runtime; 176 s for `voice-evidence`) still needs
  a warm-up before any user-facing use — and the two lanes fail differently:
  the GPU RUNTIME times out behind its broker, while `voice-evidence`, which
  has no broker, is rejected **401** because the HMAC window is 60 s
  (`hmac-skew-shorter-than-cold-start`,
  `broker-healthz-is-a-front-door-not-a-readiness-check`). The studio panel now
  owns the first of those (`preview-cold-start-is-a-state`); the processing
  worker does not yet own the second.
- **Ingestion**: the statistical half now runs on a real transcript and

  (`romanised-lexicon-meets-devanagari-asr` — deliberately unpatched, it is a

    Vercel.** The service is live and the app plane cannot reach it without
    them; both are recoverable from the container app's `listSecrets`, the same
    way §"Secret recovery" describes for the voice pair.
## The laws a new session must not relearn

- `offline-mocks-cannot-type-check-sql` — a mocked DB proves control flow, not
  types or referential integrity. Smoke-test every lane against the real
  database before calling it done.
- `aliveness-was-unreachable-not-meera-bound` — a seam can be complete at both
  ends and still be dead because nothing passes the argument between them.
  Before calling a capability "wired", grep for a CALLER, not a definition.
  database before calling it done. A mock cannot even tell you the statement
  PARSES: three shipped queries were 0A000 and had never executed once
  (`statement-shapes-postgres-will-not-parse`). EXPLAIN it — one round trip,
  no write (`explain-is-the-only-parser-we-have`).
- `coverage-lists-that-enumerate-a-subset` — a coverage check is only as wide
  as the thing it enumerates, and fixing ONE enumeration teaches you nothing
  about the others in the same query. When you widen one, list every other
  enumeration alongside it and widen or justify each.
- `gurukul-no-production-glob` — a feature branch must never match another
  product's deploy trigger.
- `track-lists-must-not-assume-a-child-exists` — a grid or flex track reserved
  for a child collapses the column the moment that child is deleted, and NO
  overflow check can see it, because a collapsed column overflows nothing. Nine
  such rules were found in `src/studio/`. Guard the rail with `:has()`, or pin
  the children to their columns explicitly. `scripts/check-layout.mjs` holds it
  and its negative control is written in its header.
- `a-check-must-be-able-to-reach-the-screen-it-judges` — the layout gate pointed
  at signed-out `/studio` measured six blocks of sign-in copy and reported OK
  against the exact bug that shipped. Assert COVERAGE in every gate, and pair
  the assertion with a way to satisfy it that needs no secret: the answer here
  was `studio-layout-fixture.html`, the real components against fixture props.
- `a-media-query-cannot-see-a-narrow-container` — the studio's content column is
  276px narrower than the viewport because the rail is beside it. Breakpoints
  written against the viewport fire in the wrong place. Use
  `repeat(auto-fit, minmax(min(100%, N), 1fr))`, which asks the container.
- `layer-order-must-survive-the-minifier` — LightningCSS strips a standalone
  `@layer a, b, c;` statement. That inverted the studio's cascade and shipped
  every primary CTA at 1.73:1 contrast. The order is declared in an inline
  `<style>` in `studio.html`'s head; if you change the layer names, change it
  there too.
- `recited-prompt` / `prompt-position` — write shapes, never lines; position is
  mechanism.
- Isolation is a SQL predicate, never a prompt instruction.
- Every claim is measured or it is marked unverified. No offline numbers in
  `measurements.md`.
- `aliveness-was-unreachable-not-meera-bound` — "is this module generic" is the
  wrong question. Name the CALL SITE that reaches it with a non-default agent;
  a grep with no hits means the feature does not exist for that agent.
- `clone-initiative-record-has-no-absence` — a clone may speak first only on a
  citable reason. Silence, gaps and streaks are not inputs the predicate HAS.
  Do not re-add a silence-triggered ping in any form, on any surface.
  `measurements.md` — with one narrow exception added 2026-08-26: a measurement
  OF A PROMPT'S OWN TEXT (`exdialog-surface`) is exact rather than a proxy, and
  is admitted with its scope line stated before the numbers. A number produced
  against a MOCKED DATABASE is still not admissible as a product measurement,
  and `surface-switch-recall` says so in its own entry.
- **Memory is never keyed by surface** (`api/_surface.js` §4). Retrieval
  violated this until 2026-08-26 and lost 89.2% of recall on a surface switch;
  the legacy FORGET lane violated it until later the same day
  (`forget-follows-the-person` closed `legacy-forget-is-device-scoped`; live
  eval `evals/forget/crosssurface.mjs --live`, 39/39 with negative controls).
- A mock branch keyed on a TABLE NAME will one day answer a different query
  than it was written for, and a mock that OVER-RETURNS hides real defects while
  every assertion stays green
  (`router-matched-a-table-instead-of-a-statement`).

END of the file on purpose: the merge helper unions append-only regions, so a
line added here by two agents at once merges cleanly, while a line added to the
header stacks up. See the header for the full reason.

- **main-session 22:20Z** — the Sarvam key is ON the Azure job (verified by GET: `SARVAM_API_KEY` -> `secretRef=sarvam-key`, five secrets intact, ten sibling env vars untouched, provisioningState Succeeded). - **WS-AR** — the "Preview my voice" `wav format unsupported` bug, confirmed and fixed at its real source: `services/voice-evidence/app.py`'s `_enhance` always emitted 48 kHz while `probeEnrollmentWav` always required 24 kHz, and 23 real dated `vy_replica_generation` rows on the owner's replica prove this failed for real, repeatedly, before this session. - **WS-R11, money behind a seam** — the durable ledger and provider seam for Vyakti Rooms revenue, spending nothing: "Payments are Phase 1 work. This workstream builds the durable ledger and the provider seam so Phase 1 can turn it on with a key." Migration 078 (`vy_room_price`, `vy_room_subscription`, `vy_payment_event`, `vy_creator_payout`; 43 statements, idempotent, no FK on owner/person, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`). `api/_payments.js` holds every decision: the price band (INR 299-599, CHECK-mirrored), the platform take (2500 bp default, copied onto each ledger row at write time rather than re-read later, `decisions.md#ws-r11-price-and-take-as-data`); `startFollowerSubscription`/`followerSubscriptionStatus` (idempotent on the follower, session-scoped exactly as `roomSay` is, reusing `api/_room-surface.js`'s `resolveRoom`/`followerRow` rather than re-deriving identity); `applyWebhook` (verify the HMAC-SHA256 signature over the RAW body FIRST, refuse before any write on failure, then one multi-CTE statement that idempotently inserts the ledger row on `(provider, provider_event_ref)`, updates the subscription's state, and flips the follower's `tier` to `'paid'` ONLY when the resulting state is `'active'` and back to `'free'` only on a terminal one — `_provider-budget.js`'s reservation shape, one file over); `runPayoutRollup` (idempotent on `(owner, period)`, TDS defaults to 0 rather than a guessed rate, `decisions.md#ws-r11-tds-rate-defaults-to-zero`). Two provider twins behind `PAYMENTS_PROVIDER` (`none` default refuses every write, named, before any provider is ever called): `api/_payments/providers/razorpay.js` (never executed live, endpoints and webhook event names pinned with the date they were read, 2026-09-03) and `fake.js` (byte-identical HMAC algorithm, zero network, what the offline suite and a staging deploy actually run against). The provider credential reuses `api/_channel-secrets.js`'s own backend seam under one fixed platform-level ref rather than a new secret store (`decisions.md#ws-r11-provider-seam-and-secret-reuse`, citing `credential-ref-not-credential`). `vy_room_subscription` is in `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES` (honest manifest coverage) but `wipeWhere`-gated to terminal states, so a live mandate survives even a full account wipe rather than being silently orphaned (`decisions.md#ws-r11-subscription-survives-forget-until-terminal`). `src/room/RoomApp.tsx` wires the existing end-of-session upgrade line and the cap screen to a real "Upgrade" action that opens the provider's own checkout page or states exactly why it cannot (no provider configured, no price set); `src/studio/RoomStudio.tsx` grew a Price card (band-bounded, same preset-pill shape as the free-cap card) and a Money card (subscribers, churned this month, this month's split, the latest payout — real counts, "No subscribers yet" on zero). `evals/payments/run.mjs`: 62/62 offline, $0, covering band enforcement, subscribe-through-the-fake-provider, byte-exact signature verification with idempotent replay, the full state machine and the tier flip, the 25% split's arithmetic (a CHECK-backed invariant), the payout roll-up, `PAYMENTS_PROVIDER=none` refusing every write, and the brief's own required negative control (the signature check's position in the source is asserted directly, and the migration's `signature_verified=true` CHECK is asserted to exist, since `applyWebhook`'s own INSERT can never pass anything but the literal `true`). Building this surfaced and fixed two real defects in SHARED evals rather than working around them: `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s blanket per-file allowlist would have silently disabled all future leak checking on `api/_payments.js` (a narrow `TIER_WRITE_ONLY` class was added instead, proven load-bearing by hand against a deliberately leaking copy — `rejected.md#ws-r11-room-leak-blanket-allowlist`), and `evals/persontables.mjs`'s `wipeWhere` identifier scan could not tell a quoted SQL string literal's contents from a column name, false-failing on `"state in ('cancelled','expired')"` (fixed generally by stripping string literals before the scan — `rejected.md#ws-r11-persontables-wipeWhere-string-literal-false-positive`). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: 14/15 untouched (the one failure was `EADDRINUSE:8931`, a concurrent sibling session's own layout-gate port collision, confirmed environmental and not this workstream's), **15/15** after. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 829 nodes, 1021 edges. NOT PROVEN: no statement in migration 078 or `api/_payments.js` has ever run against a live Postgres; no real Razorpay account, subscription, or webhook has ever existed; the paid tier's fair-use ceiling (500 messages / 30 voice minutes a month, named in the Rooms plan's own product paragraph) is not enforced anywhere in this workstream — out of the five numbered Build steps in the brief, and voice minutes have no metering surface anywhere in this codebase yet; an abandoned mandate-collection flow has no path to re-fetch a fresh checkout link (Phase 1 work, named rather than silently missing).

Eleven conflicts: four context files unioned as one side plus the other side's diff, graph by structural union (+9 nodes), and six both-added code hunks kept both ways (`api/memory.js` PERSON_TABLES, `db/schema.sql`, the recall FATE map, the leak battery's `AGGREGATE_ONLY` now `_room-publish.js` + `_room-cohorts.js` beside R11's `TIER_WRITE_ONLY`, the sqlcast strict list, `RoomStudio.tsx` with one `onAuthError` type over all three error classes). 078 applied live in one transaction; every payments statement and erasure delete `EXPLAIN`ed; the one sequential scan (follower status in any state) fixed with `vy_room_subscription_follower_ix` added to 078 and applied live (`measurements.md#rooms-migration-078-live-verification-2026-09-03`). Wave three is fully merged: R11, R12, R13, R15. 079 is the next free migration number.

- **WS-R16, check-ins** — the follower's second reason to pay: the creator's AI follows up on a task the FOLLOWER chose and scheduled, never on silence (`decisions.md#proactive-reason-contingent` restated and cited by this migration's own header). Migration 079 adds three tables: `vy_room_checkin_design` (owner lane, like `vy_room_price`, deleted by name in `api/_replica-full-erasure.js` since `scripts/relcheck.mjs`'s owner-lane walk only follows cascades starting at `vy_replica` and `vy_room` has no real FK to it — `decisions.md#ws-r16-checkins-owner-lane-explicit-not-cascade`); `vy_room_checkin` and `vy_room_checkin_delivery` (person lane, `PERSON_TABLES`, `"forget-only"` FATE verdicts, reached by `roomForget`'s own explicit room_id+person_id delete rather than the generic per-agent loop, `vy_room_follower_day`'s 077 precedent restated since neither carries an `agent_id` column). `next_due_at` is the only column any due-select statement reads, so a row with no schedule is structurally unselectable (workstream law #1). `api/_checkins.js` holds every decision: owner ops (`createDesign`/`listDesigns`/`pauseDesign`, `api/_room-cohorts.js`'s `ownedRoomHandle` shape); follower ops (`optIn`/`stop`/`listMine`/`listRoomCheckinDesigns`, a re-derived `followerScope` matching `_room-surface.js`'s unexported `selfScope`); `computeNextDue` (pure, `Intl`-based DST-aware zoned-time resolution, no dependency, tested across a DST-free IST fixture and a real America/New_York spring-forward from both sides — one known gap found and logged rather than fixed, `decisions.md#ws-r16-checkin-dst-transition-instant`); and `sweep` (two separate SQL statements over the same due rows — one whose WHERE names `f.tier = 'paid'` and drives real delivery through `gatedReply`, one whose WHERE names the complement and drives a `skipped_free_tier` ledger write plus a reschedule — so which follower ever reaches the model is decided by SQL text, never a JS branch, workstream law #2 verbatim; `decisions.md#ws-r16-memory-consent-required-at-optin` explains why `optIn` also requires memory consent, since a cron tick has no live response to hand a transcript digest to the way `roomSay`'s memory-declined path does). The combined write (`writeOutcome`) advances `next_due_at` and inserts the ledger row in ONE statement per row via a `WITH ... UPDATE ... INSERT` CTE, with the UPDATE's `next_due_at = ($3)` guard as the whole idempotency mechanism — no lock, no transaction, a racing second processor simply finds nothing to advance. `deliverers.whatsappTemplate` records intent in the same ledger with `channel='whatsapp_template'`, always writes `state='not_configured'`, and never calls Meta regardless of whether `ROOM_WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_ID`/`ROOM_WHATSAPP_NUMBER_ID` are set (out of scope to send, workstream law #5). `countDelivery(deps)` is a named no-op seam for WS-R19's future paid fair-use ceiling. `api/checkins.js` (thin, owner+follower dispatch) and `api/checkins-sweep.js` (cron, `CRON_SECRET`, `api/drift-watch-sweep.js`'s own shape, added to `vercel.json` at `*/15 * * * *`). Studio: a self-contained `CheckinsCard.tsx` (own fetch/create/pause state, mounted in `RoomStudio.tsx` rather than threading three more hooks through that file's existing graph) plus `checkinsApi.ts`. Room: a self-contained `CheckinsPanel.tsx` dialog (`DataMenu`'s own shape) opened from a new header button shown only when `tier==='paid' && remembers`, plus `roomCheckinsApi.ts`; copy centralized in `ROOM_COPY.checkins`. `evals/checkins/run.mjs`: 35/35 offline, real bundled engine, five sections (the math, the happy path through the real `gatedReply`, idempotency, three negative controls each proven with a runtime throw-if-called assertion rather than only a data check, and the seams) — see `measurements.md#ws-r16-checkins-offline-suite-2026-09-03`. `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES`/`REPLICA_PERSON_TABLES`, `evals/recall/run.mjs`'s FATE table, `evals/persontables.mjs` (offline, unmodified, passed by construction), and `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s `ALLOWED` reader set (admitting `_checkins.js` as a follower-serving file, never a creator-facing one — it reads `vy_room_follower` only to decide who gets a check-in delivered back into their own private thread) were all updated in the same change. One real defect found and fixed before any gate ran clean: a SQL comment inside `_replica-full-erasure.js`'s template literal quoted an identifier in backticks and closed the JS string early — `node --check` caught it immediately; logged as `rejected.md#ws-r16-sql-comment-backticks-terminate-the-template-literal` since this is the SECOND time this exact defect shape has been hit (WS-R2, 2026-09-03, same day). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15 on the untouched tree** and **15/15 after**. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6/6 scopes clean, 17/17 negative controls (four em-dashes in the new React copy found and fixed before this count). `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 861 nodes / 1055 edges. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: migration 079 has never been applied to any database; no real `vy_room_checkin_design`/`vy_room_checkin`/`vy_room_checkin_delivery` row exists outside a fake `db`; no statement this workstream wrote has ever been `EXPLAIN`ed against a live Postgres (eight statements listed in the final report for the main loop to run); no cron tick of `api/checkins-sweep.js` has ever executed against a real deployment; `scripts/relcheck.mjs` did not run (no `NEON_URL` in this environment). Did not push; on branch `ws-r16-checkins`.

- **WS-R17, Pulse v0** — the creator-facing costume the plan itself named the risk: "Pulse is the write-path leak in a dashboard costume." Migration 080 adds three tables: `vy_room_pulse_optin` (PERSON lane, a follower's revocable per-thread-or-whole-room toggle, content-free, no `agent_id` column - `roomForget`'s pattern one migration over), `vy_room_pulse_topic` (OWNER lane, a short creator-typed label list, capped at 8, unique per room case-insensitively), `vy_room_pulse_snapshot` (content-free and derived, `follower_count integer check (>= 5)` - the floor enforced as a DATABASE CONSTRAINT, not merely an application check). `api/_pulse.js` holds every decision: `setOptIn`/`revoke` scope entirely off the follower's session and reuse ALREADY-EXPORTED `api/_room-surface.js` functions (`resolveRoom`, `followerRow`, `listThreads`) for identity and thread-ownership rather than writing a second hand-rolled scope check; `setTopics` (owner-scoped, replaces the whole list); `topicFollowerCount` (the one statement that reaches `vy_room_thread` at all, an outer `count(*)` over a derived `distinct person_id` table whose own `exists(...)` subquery's `from` sits after the outer statement's own - `ws-r12-retention-exists-in-select-broke-the-leak-batterys-parser`'s exact lesson applied rather than relearned, and it was NOT free: a first draft used `count(distinct t.person_id)` directly and failed the same parser for the reason logged at `rejected.md#ws-r17-count-distinct-person-id-fails-the-select-list-text-scan`); `computeSnapshot` (deletes the week's rows first - recomputed, never patched - gates the WHOLE snapshot on a room-total floor before any topic query runs, drops every per-topic bucket under 5); `readPulse` (owner, two honest empty states - "not enough people have opted in yet" vs "no topic at floor" - never a fake zero); `runPulseSweep` (every published Room, the most recently closed ISO week, `api/_drift-watch.js`'s own shape). `api/pulse.js` (owner GET/POST, `api/room-cohorts.js`'s shape) and `api/pulse-sweep.js` (weekly cron, Monday 03:00 UTC, reusing the existing `CRON_SECRET` - zero new env vars). Two new follower ops on the EXISTING `/api/room` endpoint (`pulse_optin`/`pulse_revoke`, session-scoped exactly as `thread` is) rather than a new endpoint, since the credential and the scope are identical to every other follower-session op. `_pulse.js` is admitted to `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s AGGREGATE_ONLY set, and the admission is proven load-bearing three ways on this date (removing it reproduces the "no file outside the allowed set" failure; reintroducing the `count(distinct person_id)` shape reproduces a `non-aggregate-read` failure; the shipped file passes clean) - `measurements.md#ws-r17-pulse-gate-results-2026-09-03`. The battery also gained a fifth layer: a real N=5 opted-in world, real thread titles carrying unique follower tokens, `computeSnapshot` and `readPulse` run for real, every unique token scanned absent from both, plus a non-vacuity check that the tokens really are present in the raw world first. `RoomStudio.tsx` gained a Pulse card (topic chips with add/remove, the three real counts or the honest empty state) under the Week six card; `RoomApp.tsx` gained a "Let this count" toggle (local-only optimistic state, `decisions.md#ws-r17-pulse-toggle-is-local-optimistic-no-prefetch` - it fetches nothing on load, so the layout gate's fixture render needed no new stub). `evals/pulse/` is a new suite (`fixtures.mjs` extends `evals/room/fixtures.mjs`'s shared world with the three new tables by reading the real SQL text for the clauses present, the same technique that file already uses for `vy_room_thread`; `run.mjs` is 19 offline checks covering the workstream brief's own six cases a-f plus two `readPulse` states), registered in `evals/run.mjs`. `vy_room_pulse_optin` is in `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES` (`"forget-only"` in the recall FATE map, `evals/recall/run.mjs`) and `REPLICA_PERSON_TABLES`'s deploy-ordering gate; `roomForget` deletes it explicitly by room_id+person_id, gated on `isTableAppliedFor`, `vy_room_follower_day`'s exact pattern. `api/_replica-full-erasure.js` deletes all three pulse tables by name (a new `owner_room_pulse` receipt class) - snapshot and topic reached via the same room_id subquery `payment_events`/`room_subscriptions` already use, optin the same way. **One real bug found and fixed during this session's own gate run, not merely avoided**: the erasure-job comment added alongside those three new CTEs used backtick characters inside a SQL `--` comment that itself lives inside a backtick-delimited JS template literal, closing the JS string early and producing `SyntaxError: missing ) after argument list` - caught by `node evals/run.mjs`'s `replicaerasure` suite (tsc does not check `.js` files, so `typecheck` passed while this was broken), fixed by removing the backticks from the comment, confirmed by re-running the suite (20/20) and by `node -e "import(...)"` on every touched `.js` file. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15 on the untouched tree** (confirmed via `git reset --hard 844d9d5`); after this workstream's changes, every gate up through `layout readability` passed on a direct run, and `eval suite` (`node evals/run.mjs`, exit 0) and `room leak battery` (67/67) were independently confirmed by running them standalone rather than waiting out the full `verify-release.mjs` process, which was still running when this entry was written under heavy CPU contention from several concurrent sibling sessions on the same 4-core machine (load average above 6, multiple other worktrees' own `verify-release.mjs`/`evals/run.mjs` visible in `ps aux` at the same timestamps) - see the final report for the exact commands run and their exit codes. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 858 nodes, 1056 edges. - **WS-R18, the Room on Telegram** — a transport, never a tenant, over the SAME follower lane the web Room uses. `api/_room-telegram.js` (state machine: `/start <slug>` sends the disclosure card once then an inline-keyboard age question then a memory question, both answers landing in ONE `joinRoom` call; ordinary text reaches `roomSay` only for a chat with a bound pointer AND an attested follower row, else the app-voiced "open a Room first" card and never a creator-voiced reply; `/forget` `/export` `/stop`, the last removing only the chat's Room pointer, no deletion) and a thin `api/room-tg.js`. Identity is `vy_surface_identity` via `personForSurfaceUser`/`linkSurfacePerson` (`api/_room.js`, the exact bridge `api/tg.js` already uses for Meera) — no second identity system. `openRoom`/`joinRoom` (`api/_room-surface.js`) gained an additive `personId` bypass (tried before the Supabase `authUserId` bridge; every existing caller unaffected) so the Telegram lane reuses the follower lane's own SQL rather than copying it, which is also why migration 082's raw SQL lives in `api/_room-surface.js` (`bindTelegramChannel`/`telegramChannelRoom`/`unbindTelegramChannel`) rather than the new Telegram file — `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s repo-wide table-name scan only allowlists that file for follower-table SQL, and `vy_room_follower_channel` contains `vy_room_follower` as a literal substring. Migration 082 (`vy_room_follower_channel`: room_id, person_id, follower_id, channel, channel_ref, unique on `(channel, channel_ref)`, `follower_id` cascading) exists because `ROOM_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` is ONE bot for the whole platform (not a per-creator credential), so one Telegram chat can mean different creators' Rooms over time and an ordinary message carries no slug — this is the only fact reuse could not already answer. `api/_room-publish.js`'s `clientRoom` gained `telegram_deep_link` (`https://t.me/<ROOM_TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME>?start=<slug>`, `null` when unconfigured — the server's own honest "not connected"), rendered in `RoomStudio.tsx`. Three new env vars, all read directly via `process.env` (not baked into `api/_config.js`, `PAYMENTS_PROVIDER`'s own pattern): `ROOM_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN`, `ROOM_TELEGRAM_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (unset answers every webhook 503 `room_telegram_unconfigured`, never a silent 200; a configured-but-wrong secret is 401, constant-time, before any db read), `ROOM_TELEGRAM_BOT_USERNAME`. `evals/room-telegram/run.mjs`: 51/51, four required negative controls (unjoined chat never gets a creator-voiced reply, model call count 0; wrong/unset secret refused by a function proven to take no `db` parameter; group update refused by name with a poisoned `db` proving no read follows; two Telegram followers zero cross-follower tokens, with the detector proven capable of failing first via a rigged reply, `evals/room-leak`'s own required shape reused). Building it surfaced three real defects, all logged because none was guessable in advance: `personForSurfaceUser`/`linkSurfacePerson` call `q()` directly and are NOT db-injectable the way every `_room-surface.js` function is, so the first eval draft silently misreported every join as a refused minor (`rejected.md#ws-r18-personforsurfaceuser-is-not-db-injectable`); a naive fake-db branch placement would have let the OLDER `vy_room_follower` branch swallow the new table's statements, since the new name contains the old one as a substring (`rejected.md#ws-r18-fake-db-branch-would-have-swallowed-the-channel-table`); and the hand-rolled fake db does not enact migration 082's `ON DELETE CASCADE` automatically, so `roomForget`'s shared fixture branch needed an explicit line simulating it (`rejected.md#ws-r18-fake-db-does-not-simulate-postgres-fk-cascade`). Scoped out and named rather than silently missing: a Telegram follower who declines memory gets no cross-turn continuity (a fresh empty transcript minted every message — there is no browser tab to hold a running one on Telegram, `decisions.md#ws-r18-telegram-memory-decline-has-no-cross-turn-continuity`); the capped card names that a paid plan exists when `PAYMENTS_PROVIDER` is configured but does not attempt an inline Telegram checkout flow; `scripts/first-room.mjs` gained no `--telegram` step (verifying it costs nothing would itself require a live run this session could not make, so it was left rather than guessed at); no thread/history commands (only `/forget` `/export` `/stop`, the three the brief names). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15**, run once on the tree with this workstream's full changeset already applied — this session did not capture a separate untouched-tree baseline as its own first step (see `measurements.md#ws-r18-room-telegram-gate-results-2026-09-03` for the honest accounting of that gap and the indirect evidence in its place). Every sibling Room suite (`room` 54/54, `room-leak` 62/62 incl. both static layers, `room-publish` 39/39 with 2 new deep-link checks, `payments` 62/62, `room-cohorts` 60/60, `recall` 242 assertions incl. the new table's FATE verdict) re-run unchanged. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean. See `context/decisions.md` (`ws-r18-*`, four entries), `context/rejected.md` (`ws-r18-*`, three entries), `context/measurements.md#ws-r18-room-telegram-gate-results-2026-09-03`. NOT PROVEN: migration 082 has never executed against a database; no statement in it has been `EXPLAIN`ed; `scripts/relcheck.mjs` did not run (no `NEON_URL`); no real Telegram update has ever reached `api/room-tg.js` — code-complete and offline-proven only, same status `docs/SURFACES.md` already gives Discord and WhatsApp.

- **WS-R19, the paid tier's fair-use ceilings and voice replies** — closed the gap WS-R11's own report named: neither ceiling in "unlimited-feeling chat under a fair-use ceiling of 500 messages a month, 30 voice minutes a month" was enforced anywhere, and voice had no metering surface at all. Migration 081 (`db/migrations/081_room_paid_tier.sql`, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`): `vy_room_follower` gains `voice_seconds_month` and its OWN rollover key `voice_month_key` (deliberately separate from 071's `month_key` — see `decisions.md#ws-r19-shared-month-key-cross-counter-rollover`, a real cross-counter rollover defect this workstream's own offline eval caught, not a stylistic choice); `vy_room` gains `paid_monthly_messages` (default 500, CHECK 100-2000) and `paid_monthly_voice_seconds` (default 1800, CHECK 0-3600), both creator-editable columns exactly as 071's `free_monthly_messages` already is; `vy_room_voice_usage` is 077's `vy_room_follower_day` one column deeper (a real FK to `vy_room_follower`), content-free, PERSON lane, `"forget-only"` FATE, gated in `activePersonTables()`/`REPLICA_PERSON_TABLES`. `api/_room-surface.js`'s `roomSay` cap UPDATE became one CASE on `f.tier` spending against `r.paid_monthly_messages` or `r.free_monthly_messages` in the identical predicate shape, naming which ceiling was hit from the `followerRow` read (`room_paid_cap_reached` vs `room_free_cap_reached`) since an empty UPDATE result carries no tier of its own. New `roomSpeak(deps, session, replyRef)`: paid-tier-only (refused at the door, before any hash or cap check), bound to the EXACT reply text `roomSay` last delivered (a session-carried hash, `payload.lr`, so voice can only ever render a reply that already passed `gatedReply` — never a second way to make this AI say something), spends `voice_seconds_month` in one conditional UPDATE before any synthesis, authorizes through the EXISTING `beginOwnedVoicePreview` (new glue file `api/_room-voice.js`, never a new authorization path — `decisions.md#ws-r19-voice-reuses-the-preview-ledger-shape` explains why migration 045's own CHECK makes `purpose='voice_preview'`/`channel='studio_preview'` the ONLY schema-compatible choice for a row drift watch's swap sweep can see), and returns audio that can only ever have come from `deps.protect`'s output — structurally, there is no variable in scope past the collection loop that could name the provider's raw stream instead. `api/room.js`'s `speak` op (behind `ROOM_VOICE=1`, else the same `unknown_op` any absent capability gets) wires the REAL provider and protection adapters `api/voice-preview.js` already uses, so with no `AZURE_OPEN_VOICE_ORIGIN` configured in this environment it 503s "not configured" exactly like the studio panel already does — no GPU wake occurred or could occur in this session. Studio: `api/_room-publish.js`'s `setRoomPaidCeilings` (a `RoomStudio.tsx` card, band-bounded number inputs). Room: a paid-only play control per reply bubble and a real voice-minutes line in the follower's own menu, both behind `VITE_ROOM_VOICE`. `evals/room-paid-tier/run.mjs`: 38/38 offline — both ceilings at their exact boundary, the voice cap charged before synthesis, month rollover resetting both counters independently, and three negative controls (a free follower's `roomSpeak` refused with zero synth/protect calls; a source-level strike of the audio-collection line, run as a real dynamic-imported copy, proven to leak raw bytes the real module's own passing check would have caught; a static proof that the ledger row's two literals match drift watch's own filter, with a diverged copy shown to disagree). Building this found and fixed a real defect in a SHARED fixture: the CASE-on-tier rewrite silently broke `evals/room/fixtures.mjs`'s substring-matched fake cap (three suites share it), which would have let `evals/room/run.mjs` and `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` keep passing while enforcing NOTHING (`rejected.md#ws-r19-paid-cap-case-broke-the-shared-room-fixture`) — fixed and both suites reconfirmed (54/54, 62/62 with 16,080 retrieval checks). - **WS-R21, the ops board and sweep heartbeats** — Phase 0 is one creator and 100 followers watched by one person with no screen today; this closes that. Migration 084 (`vy_sweep_run`: run_id, sweep, started_at, finished_at, outcome running/ok/partial/failed, counts jsonb capped at 4096 bytes, error_code) - no person/owner column by construction, so it needs no `PERSON_TABLES` entry, no erasure wiring and no `scripts/relcheck.mjs` exemption (it carries none of that check's `PERSON_COLUMNS`, invisible to the coverage scan by construction rather than by an added exception). `api/_sweep-run.js`'s `withSweepRun(db, sweep, fn)` wraps a sweep's own work: INSERTs the row at start (`outcome='running'`), UPDATEs the SAME row at finish - a hard-killed invocation past its own `maxDuration` therefore leaves a row permanently stuck at `'running'`, which IS the honest signal, rather than write-once-at-the-end leaving no row at all (`decisions.md#ws-r21-heartbeat-write-at-start-and-finish`). `sanitizeCounts` reduces a sweep's own return value to numbers/booleans one level deep (arrays collapse to their length); every string and nested object is dropped, never stringified, proven load-bearing against this repo's own sweep summaries that already carry free text (`api/_pulse.js`'s `error_details: [{room_id, message}]`) - `decisions.md#ws-r21-sanitize-counts-drops-content-never-stringifies`. Every one of the 11 crons in `vercel.json` is wired through it (`drift-watch`, `checkins`, `pulse`, `replica-voice-identity`, `replica-liveness`, `replica-identity`, `replica-face-session`, `replica-model-build`, `channel-ingest`, `replica-erasure`, `consolidate`) with one deliberate exception: `consolidate-sweep.js`'s `CONSOLIDATE_KILL` early return stays outside the wrapper, honoring that switch's own already-shipped "nothing written" comment literally (`decisions.md#ws-r21-consolidate-kill-switch-writes-no-heartbeat`). `api/_sweep-schedule.js` reads `vercel.json` itself (a static JSON import, `api/route.js`'s own `config/models.json` convention) rather than a hand-maintained second schedule table, and parses the every-N-minutes/every-N-hours/hourly/one-weekly-slot shapes this repo's own crons actually use - an unrecognised shape returns `null`, never a guessed number. `api/_ops.js`'s `opsOverview(db, now)` returns, per Room: followers total/paid/joined-last-7d, messages last 24h (`vy_room_follower_day`), followers at this month's message cap (compared against `vy_room`'s own `free_monthly_messages`/`paid_monthly_messages`, never a hardcoded ceiling), voice seconds this month, active check-ins, deliveries last 24h by state, Pulse opt-ins and latest week (reusing `api/_pulse.js`'s own `readPulse` rather than re-deriving it), subscriptions by state, revenue this month (`vy_payment_event`), latest drift report state; plus a sweeps strip joining `vy_sweep_run`'s latest row per sweep against the schedule table, flagging `stale` past 2x the expected interval, `never_ran` (not `'ok'`) for a sweep with no row at all. Every follower-table statement is scoped to ONE room (`where room_id = $1`, never grouped across rooms) so its select list is pure `count()`/`sum()`, `api/_room-cohorts.js`'s own precedent and trade for the identical reason (`decisions.md#ws-r21-per-room-scoped-queries-not-grouped-across-rooms`); `api/_ops.js` is admitted to `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s `AGGREGATE_ONLY` class, proven load-bearing with two negative-control mutations (a bare `person_id` and a `message_text` appended to the real select list, both caught by the same parser, `measurements.md#ws-r21-ops-board-gate-results-2026-09-04`). `api/ops.js` (thin GET) enforces `OPS_OWNER_USER_IDS` (comma-separated Supabase user ids): unset or a non-listed caller both get 404, never 403, checked before any database read (`decisions.md#ws-r21-platform-operator-404-not-403`) - the same bearer path `api/replica.js` uses, never a second auth system. `src/studio/OpsBoard.tsx` mounts standalone from `main.tsx` on `?mode=ops` (its own small Google-sign-in flow reusing `./session.ts`/`./studioAuth.ts`, never a StudioApp mode) rather than inside `StudioApp.tsx`, so `scripts/check-layout.mjs`'s real signed-in render (a separate harness importing `StudioApp` directly) is untouched; a table per Room, tabular numbers, a sweeps strip with a stale sweep in the amber `--state-waiting` token, honest empty states (a zero-follower Room shows real zeros; a never-run sweep says so), refresh on pointerdown. `evals/ops/run.mjs`: 62/62 offline, five sections (the allowlist, the schedule table against every real cron, the heartbeat's start/finish/content-free guarantees, `opsOverview`'s real counts over two Rooms - one populated, one empty, and the four required negative controls a-d). Reused rather than re-derived: `evals/room/fixtures.mjs`'s `fakeDb`/`freshState` and `evals/pulse/fixtures.mjs`'s `pulseDb`, layered exactly as `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s own layer 5 already does. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15 on the untouched tree and 15/15 after** (`measurements.md#ws-r21-ops-board-gate-results-2026-09-04`); `node evals/room-leak/run.mjs` standalone: **67/67** (up from 62/62, +5 assertions from this workstream's own `§1c` additions). `node scripts/context.mjs --check` and `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: both clean. **A genuine hazard found and recovered from, worth any future session reading before reaching for `git stash` in a worktree**: `refs/stash` is shared across every `git worktree` of the same clone, and a concurrent sibling session's own stash push/pop raced this one mid-gate-run, applying another workstream's ("ws-r23-creator-invites") uncommitted WIP into this session's directory when this session tried to pop its own; recovered cleanly with no data lost from either side (full account and the recovery method: `rejected.md#ws-r21-git-stash-is-shared-across-concurrent-worktree-sessions`). Migration 085 (`db/migrations/085_room_push.sql`, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`): `vy_room_push_subscription` (PERSON lane, PERSON_TABLES, `"forget-only"` FATE, no `agent_id` column, reached purely by `follower_id references vy_room_follower(follower_id) on delete cascade` — `vy_room_follower_channel`'s (WS-R18) exact precedent, so `roomForget` needed no new explicit statement); `vy_room_checkin_delivery`'s channel CHECK widened idempotently (drop-then-add) to admit `'web_push'`; `vy_room_checkin` gains nullable `quiet_from`/`quiet_to` (`time`), both null by default. `api/_push/webpush.js` implements RFC 8291 (aes128gcm) + RFC 8292 (VAPID ES256 JWT) directly over `node:crypto` — no new dependency, per the brief's own instruction — proven by round-tripping the encoder's output through an independently-written decoder against a freshly generated real P-256 keypair; a memorized RFC 8291 Appendix A known-answer vector was tried first and rejected when the transcribed public key failed to parse as a valid curve point (`rejected.md#ws-r22-rfc-8291-known-answer-vector-from-memory`), so real-browser/push-service interop remains unproven, stated plainly rather than implied. `checkinPushPayload(slug, displayName, threadId)`'s own parameter list is the enforcement of workstream law #1 (never the check-in text): the room slug, the creator's PUBLIC display name and a thread id, nothing else, statically checked in the offline suite with a required negative control proving the scanner catches a poisoned version. `api/_room-push.js` (subscribe/unsubscribe/status, scoped off the follower's own session, `api/_pulse.js`'s re-derived-`followerScope` shape) and two new ops on the existing `/api/room` endpoint: `push_subscribe`/`push_unsubscribe`/`push_status`. `api/_checkins.js` gains `deliverers.webPush` (called from `deliverOne` only AFTER the in-app delivery has claimed the row; one push per active subscription; ONE ledger row per occurrence — `delivered`/`failed`/`not_configured`, migration 079's own unique-per-channel constraint precludes one row per device, `decisions.md#ws-r22-web-push-ledger-one-row-per-occurrence`; a 404/410 revokes that subscription) and quiet-hours math in `computeNextDue` (a picked time inside the follower's own window shifts to the window's end, same day or the next for a wraparound window — `decisions.md#ws-r22-quiet-hours-shift-not-skip`) plus a matching SQL predicate on both due-select statements as defense in depth. The VAPID public key is served server-side on `api/checkins.js`'s existing `designs` op response (`push_public_key`, null when `ROOM_PUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC` is unset) rather than a client build-time env var, so the client's own key can never drift from the server's (`decisions.md#ws-r22-push-key-served-from-designs-op-not-build-time-env`). Room: `public/room.webmanifest` + `public/room-sw.js` (content-free notification body, "`<Name>` AI has a check-in for you", tap opens `/r/<slug>`), an "Allow check-ins on this phone" control plus quiet-hours pickers added to `CheckinsPanel.tsx`, and `RoomApp.tsx` swaps the manifest `<link>` to a per-room Blob URL once it knows the slug (the Room is multi-tenant; one static manifest cannot name a slug — `decisions.md#ws-r22-dynamic-manifest-blob-url-per-room`, unproven against a real "Add to Home Screen" flow). Three new env vars, all read directly via `process.env` (`ROOM_WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_ID`'s own pattern): `ROOM_PUSH_VAPID_PUBLIC`, `ROOM_PUSH_VAPID_PRIVATE`, `ROOM_PUSH_VAPID_SUBJECT` (a `mailto:`); all three unset means the seam records `not_configured` and the Room hides the enable control entirely. `evals/room-push/run.mjs`: 43/43 offline, six sections (crypto round-trip, VAPID JWT shape, quiet-hours math over a plain and a wraparound window, subscribe/unsubscribe scoped to the caller's own follower row with B unable to revoke A's subscription, the delivery ledger's four states, and the two required negative controls — (b) a 410 revokes and a second sweep sends nothing to it, (c) a world check proving A's delivery never reaches or touches B's subscription — plus the static negative control (a) on the payload builder). This session found and fixed a real setup gap in itself, not in the product: this worktree's own `npm install` had never been run, so its local `node_modules` held no real packages and every gate that constructs an explicit `node_modules/...` path (rather than relying on Node's own upward resolution, which `npx` uses and therefore misleadingly passed) failed with a literal, reproducible `MODULE_NOT_FOUND` — confirmed by direct reproduction, not assumed as CPU contention, then fixed by running the install `context/measurements.md#ws-r22-gate-results-2026-09-04` documents honestly. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15** after that fix (no separate valid untouched-tree baseline exists for this session, stated rather than fabricated). `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6/6 scopes clean, 17/17 negative controls. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 897 nodes, 1102 edges. Regression-checked unchanged: `evals/checkins/run.mjs` 35/35, `evals/room/run.mjs` 54/54, `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` 67/67, `evals/persontables.mjs` 53 manifest entries, `evals/recall/run.mjs` 257 assertions. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: migration 085 has never executed against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; nine statements listed in the final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `vy_room_push_subscription` row exists outside a fake `db`; the exact RFC 8291 Appendix A ciphertext was never independently reproduced; no real push has ever reached a real browser or push service; no real "Add to Home Screen" install has been exercised. Did not push; on branch `ws-r22-web-push`.

- **WS-R23, creator applications and invites** — closed the gap the Phase 0 runbook's own step 1 ("Application") named: a real, coded flow now gates who can create a replica, where before any signed-in account could. Migration 086 (`db/migrations/086_creator_invites.sql`, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`): `vy_creator_application` (name, archive_link, audience, contact, plus `contact_key`/`applied_on` — a lowercased contact and a UTC day both computed in JS rather than in a functional index, since a timestamptz-to-date cast is not IMMUTABLE and Postgres refuses to index it — `decisions.md#ws-r23-application-rate-limit-is-a-plain-column-not-a-functional-index`), the PLATFORM's own lane (no auth_user_id/owner_user_id/person_id column at all, so it is correctly invisible to `api/memory.js`'s PERSON_TABLES and to `scripts/relcheck.mjs`'s coverage scan — a prospective creator has not consented to anything this system tracks about a person yet), a real `on conflict (contact_key, applied_on) do nothing` unique index enforcing one application per contact per day; `vy_creator_invite` (code_hash sha256, issued_to_contact, issued_by_user_id, application_id nullable, expires_at, redeemed_at, redeemed_by_user_id) on the OWNER lane exactly as `vy_creator_payout` is — `redeemed_by_user_id` IS the replica owner's id once a code is spent — reached by a named delete in `api/_replica-full-erasure.js` (keyed on owner_user_id) rather than through the person manifest, and `scripts/relcheck.mjs`'s own `PERSON_COLUMNS`/owner-lane walk widened to recognize `redeemed_by_user_id` alongside `owner_user_id` so this table gets the same CHECKED (not merely asserted) erasure-reach guarantee every other owner-keyed table gets (`evals/persontables.mjs`'s offline mirror needed its own fix too, a written EXEMPT entry rather than a wider `ownerLane()` — `rejected.md#ws-r23-owner-lane-column-name-is-a-second-blind-spot`). `api/_apply.js` (public, `submitApplication`/`listApplications`/`eraseApplicationsByContact`) + `api/apply.js`; `api/_invites.js` (operator-only via `OPS_OWNER_USER_IDS`, the same env var name WS-R21 uses, coordinated by name only since that tree is not merged here — `issueInvite`/`listInvites`/`revokeInvite`/`eraseInvite`, plus `hashInviteCode`/`canonicalizeInviteCode` so a code is punctuation- and case-insensitive on redemption) + `api/invites.js`. `api/_replica.js`'s `createSelfReplica` gained the actual gate: a CTE that redeems the invite (an UPDATE guarded by `redeemed_at is null and expires_at > now()`) and only then permits the `vy_replica` INSERT to run (`where gate.ok`, and `gate.ok` itself depends on that SAME statement's own `invite_redeem` output) — never a JS check before or after a separate INSERT, workstream law #3 verbatim, `decisions.md#ws-r23-invite-predicate-inside-the-insert`. `INVITES_REQUIRED=1` turns the gate on; unset (today's state on every deployment) leaves every statement byte-identical to before this workstream, proven by a dedicated negative control. An account that already owns a replica needs no code for a second one. Named refusals `invite_required` (no code offered at all, a fast pre-check before the CTE runs) and `invite_invalid` (a code was offered and did not redeem — wrong, already spent, or expired, deliberately undifferentiated from each other). Studio: `InviteGate.tsx`, shown before `CreateReplicaCard` only when `VITE_INVITES_REQUIRED=1` and the account owns zero replicas, reusing `CreateReplicaCard`'s own markup/CSS rather than inventing new chrome; `replicaApi.ts`'s `createReplica` gained an optional `inviteCode` argument. `site/vyakti.html`'s two "Apply for one of the first Rooms" mailto links became a real in-page form (`#apply`, plain HTML plus a small inline script — this file's own no-build-step rule) posting to `api/apply.js`. `evals/invites/run.mjs`: 57/57 offline (`measurements.md#ws-r23-invites-offline-eval-2026-09-04`), including three negative controls (a redeemed code cannot be redeemed twice; an expired code refuses by name; with `INVITES_REQUIRED` unset the predicate is structurally absent) and a static source-text proof that the gate lives inside the INSERT. **A `git stash` collision cost real work mid-session**: every worktree in this repo shares one `.git`, the stash stack is global, and a sibling agent's concurrent `git stash pop` consumed this session's own stash entry — already documented (`rejected.md#stash-in-a-shared-git-dir`, WS-AE, 2026-08-26) and this session hit the identical failure anyway before reading it closely enough. Fully recovered with no data loss: the popped stash's two commits (`4a486699`, the tracked-file diff and its untracked-files parent `59200d6b`) stayed in the object store as unreachable commits, found via `git fsck --unreachable --no-reflogs` and recovered with `git checkout <commit> -- <paths>`; the full gate was re-run and reconfirmed 15/15 after recovery. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15 on the untouched tree** (baseline captured via a WIP commit rather than a second stash) and **15/15 after**. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 17 negative controls bit. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 895 nodes, 1102 edges. - **WS-R20, Handoff v0** — a follower asks for the human, and nothing moves without a verbatim payload screen. Migration 083 (`db/migrations/083_room_handoff.sql`, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`): `vy_room` gains `handoff_enabled` (boolean, default false, the creator's own per-Room switch) and `handoff_monthly_cap` (default 5, CHECK 0-50, 071/081's own creator-editable-column shape); `vy_room_handoff` is the one deliberate exception to 071's "never a word" law — it holds `payload_text`/`payload_sha256`/`reply_text` on purpose, because the whole mechanism IS a follower seeing exact bytes before they cross the boundary. `api/_handoff.js` (owner: `getHandoffConfig`/`setHandoffConfig`/`handoffQueue`/`answerHandoff`; follower: `draftHandoffPayload` (pure — no write; message-pick reads the follower's OWN turns via the same `memory.history` seam `roomSay` uses, filtered to `role==="user"`, never the AI's), `sendHandoffRequest` (the cap AND the enabled switch are predicates inside the INSERT's own SELECT, `_checkins.js`'s own law #2 restated for a write), `withdrawHandoffRequest`, `myHandoffs`) and a thin `api/handoff.js`. THE CONSENTED-ONLY PREDICATE: the owner's queue and answer both require `payload_sha256 = encode(digest(payload_text,'sha256'),'hex')` recomputed by Postgres on every read/write, never trusted from the insert — `api/_disclosure.js`'s own "a predicate, not a promise" restated for a consent boundary. THE CREATOR'S REPLY NEVER TOUCHES `meera_log`: `dmHistory` maps every non-`"her"` row to `"user"` with no third bucket, so any role value written there would misattribute the reply to either the AI or the follower; `reply_text` lives only in `vy_room_handoff` and is surfaced to the follower through a separate `mine` read the client merges into the thread view, tagged distinctly, never fed to any compile (`decisions.md#ws-r20-creator-reply-never-touches-meera-log`, which also names what this deliberately leaves undone: the AI citing "what `<Name>` told you" needs its own retrieval wiring, not built here). No tier gate anywhere — the brief's own "Paid only? No" (`decisions.md#ws-r20-handoff-not-tier-gated`). Room UI: "Ask `<Name>` directly" (shown only when `openRoom` reports `room.handoff_enabled`, a real column read, never a client guess), a pick-your-own-messages-or-write-a-note screen, a verbatim confirmation screen rendering the exact returned bytes in a `<pre>` block before the one Send button, and a status list with withdraw. Migration 088 (`vy_replica_funnel_mark`: replica_id/owner_user_id/step/at, primary key on (replica_id, step), no foreign key on 009's owner-lane convention, no PERSON_TABLES entry needed - picked up automatically by `evals/persontables.mjs`'s `ownerLane()` the same way `vy_replica_readiness` already is, confirmed by direct measurement: 71 owner-lane tables, up from 70). `api/_funnel.js` holds every decision: `markStep` (owner op, the ownership check lives INSIDE the INSERT's own row source via a CTE - `gate0-structural`'s shape restated - so a mark from another owner inserts zero rows in the SAME statement, never a JS check before or after one; first-write-wins via `on conflict (replica_id, step) do nothing`), `replicaFunnel` (the twelve-step ordered funnel - account_created, studio_opened, first_source_uploaded, processing_finished, first_preview_heard, readiness_first_measured, readiness_passed_lock, disclosure_approved, room_created, publish_clicked, room_published, first_follower_joined - each read from its own table with a scoped select; "processing finished" reads the audio DAG's own terminal step, `voice_quality` complete, from `api/_replica-processing/pipeline.js`'s own `NEXT` map, never re-derived from `vy_replica_source.state`), `funnelSummary` (pure: median/p90 minutes-to-publish over replicas that published, rank-based percentile with linear interpolation; stall counts by last-reached step over replicas unpublished and at least 7 days old, so a two-day-old in-progress replica is never reported as a defect), and `opsFunnel` (the board's own read, looping `replicaFunnel` over every non-purging replica - `api/_ops.js`'s own per-room loop shape one file over, the honest tradeoff at Phase 0 scale against a grouped statement that would have to group `vy_room_follower` across rooms, which this file's own law and `_ops.js`'s both forbid). The one follower-table read (`min(joined_at) from vy_room_follower where room_id = ($1)::uuid`) is admitted to `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s AGGREGATE_ONLY class, which needed widening to accept `min(...)` alongside `count(...)`/`sum(...)` first - the parser's own honest definition of "aggregate", not a special case (`decisions.md#ws-r25-aggregate-only-parser-widened-to-admit-min`); room-leak reconfirmed 78/78 unchanged. `api/replica.js` gains the `funnel_mark` op; the studio marks `studio_opened` from the SAME per-replica mount effect that already fires the enrollment/runtime/sheet reads, and `RoomStudio.tsx`'s `publish` callback marks `publish_clicked` before calling `publishOwnedRoom` (fire-and-forget, `.catch(() => {})`, since a failed mark must never block the real flow). `api/_ops.js`'s `opsOverview` gained one field, `funnel: await opsFunnel(db, now)`, so the board's one endpoint carries the one number; `OpsBoard.tsx` gained a "Minutes to first Room" card (median/p90/n, the honest "No creator has published yet" empty state) and a "Where creators stop" table (plain-words step labels, the honest "No creator has stalled for 7 days or more" empty state), and the sweeps strip now says its own retention window in plain words. Closed WS-R21's own open item: `api/_sweep-run.js`'s `withSweepRun` now runs one bounded `delete from vy_sweep_run where sweep = $1 and started_at < now() - (30 * interval '1 day')` after every finish (success or failure), planned on the existing `(sweep, started_at desc)` index, no new index, no migration. `evals/funnel/run.mjs` (new suite, 49/49 offline, $0): markStep's happy path/first-write-wins/unknown-step refusal, replicaFunnel's full ordered read over a fully-published fixture (23 minutes) and a 10-day-stalled one, funnelSummary's pure math (n=1 median=p90=23; a 4-point set giving median 25/p90 37; the young-replica non-stall case), opsFunnel driving both fixtures at once and excluding a purging replica, and three required NEGATIVE CONTROLS: (a) a mark from another owner throws `replica_not_found` and the marks table is provably untouched; (b) evals/room-leak's own aggregate-only parser (copied inline, that file exports no entry point, by design) catches a mutated select list carrying a bare follower column, in two shapes; (c) the retention delete removes an old row for the sweep that just finished while leaving an equally old row belonging to a different sweep untouched, and also fires on the failure path. `vy_replica_funnel_mark` deleted by name in `api/_replica-full-erasure.js` (same shape as 073's readiness CTE, no new receipt class - content-free tables here do not automatically get one, readiness/drift-watch's own precedent); `evals/replica-erasure/run.mjs` reconfirmed 20/20 unchanged. `evals/sqlcast/surface.mjs` gained `api/_funnel.js` on the strict list from its first commit; `sqlcast` 155 tables, 371 strict statements, 0 violations. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15 on the untouched tree and 15/15 after** (`measurements.md#ws-r25-funnel-gate-results-2026-09-04`). `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6/6 scopes clean, 17/17 negative controls, unchanged. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 930 nodes, 1146 edges (baseline 923/1139 before this session's own append). No rejections to log this session: nothing tried here broke on first run.
