- **WS-R24, the Room in Hindi** — the Room's chrome (the app-voiced cards, buttons, panels, the disclosure line, the capped card, the check-ins/Pulse/Handoff panels, the Telegram commands' replies) now speaks the follower's own language; the creator's AI is untouched and never translated. Migration 087 (`db/migrations/087_room_locale.sql`, mirrored into `db/schema.sql`): `vy_room_follower` gains `locale` (default `'en'`, CHECK `in ('en','hi')`, set at INSERT time and deliberately excluded from `joinRoom`'s `ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE` so a repeat join can never silently undo a `roomSetLocale` change); `vy_room` gains `default_locale` (same CHECK, the creator's own fallback for a follower with no row yet and no usable browser hint). `src/room/copy.ts` is now `ROOM_COPY_TABLE` (`en`/`hi`, 107 leaf paths per locale, checked equal by `evals/room-locale/run.mjs` against the real export); the three components that read it (`RoomApp.tsx`, `CheckinsPanel.tsx`, `HandoffPanel.tsx`) take a `copy` prop rather than importing a fixed English object, and `RoomApp.tsx` gained a `LanguageSwitch` (both language names shown always, in both locales) plus `lang={locale}` on every shell for `room.css`'s new `:lang(hi)` Devanagari font stack. `api/_room-surface.js`: `roomDisclosureCard(name, locale)` now has a Hindi branch (the same three checkable facts, never-deny-AI first); `openRoom`/`joinRoom` compute the effective locale (joined follower's own row, else a validated browser/Telegram hint, else the room's `default_locale`) and return it; a new `roomSetLocale` op is scoped entirely off the verified session (room/person/agent all from the token, never a request field) and mints a fresh session bound to the new card's digest. **A real bug was found and fixed before it shipped, logged at `rejected.md#ws-r24-disclosure-recomputed-from-the-follower-row-broke-every-session-across-a-switch`**: the first draft recomputed the disclosure digest from the follower row's CURRENT locale rather than the locale the session token was actually minted against, which refused every outstanding session across a language switch; fixed by giving the session payload its own `loc` field, `dd`/`td`'s own convention. `api/_room-telegram.js`: every app-voiced card takes a `locale`, Telegram's own `language_code` picks it before a follower row exists, `/hindi` and `/english` are new commands answered through the same `roomSetLocale`. `scripts/check-copy.mjs`'s rooms-vocabulary rule gained the Hindi bans (क्लोन/मॉडल/प्रतिकृति), and its `textNodes()` extractor was widened to recognize Devanagari letters as content, not only `[A-Za-z]` — a real, measured blind spot (`measurements.md#ws-r24-textnodes-devanagari-blind-spot`): before the fix a pure-Devanagari text node with a banned word inside it produced ZERO offences, in ANY rule. `scripts/check-layout.mjs` gained a `room-hi` target (the same two screens, chrome in Hindi) via a `?lang=hi` query on the existing Room layout fixture. Studio: a "Room language" card (`RoomStudio.tsx`, `api/_room-publish.js`'s new `setRoomDefaultLocale`) sets the creator's own default. `evals/room-locale/run.mjs`: 44/44 offline, $0, including the three required negative controls (a Hindi em-dash fails the dash rule; two variants of a Hindi banned word fail rooms-vocabulary; the compiled prompt handed to the model is byte-identical whatever the follower's chrome locale is, proven dynamically by capturing what actually reaches `deps.reply` on an en-locale and a hi-locale run of the SAME follower and diffing them). `evals/room/fixtures.mjs` (shared by five other suites) was extended additively to track the two new columns; every suite that shares it was re-run and is unchanged: `room` 54/54, `room-leak` 78 assertions (16,096 retrieval + 452 boundary, 0 leaks), `checkins` 35/35, `pulse` 19/19, `room-cohorts` 60/60, `room-publish` 39/39, `handoff` 30/30, `room-push` 43/43, `room-telegram` 51/51, `payments` 62/62. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/15** both before and after (2 relational DB gates skipped, no `NEON_URL` in this environment); `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6/6 scopes, 21/21 negative controls; `npx tsc -b`: clean; `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 933 nodes / 1149 edges. **Deliberately not touched, and logged as a decision rather than an oversight** (`decisions.md#ws-r24-room-card-in-api-surface-js-is-not-vyakti-rooms`): both this workstream's own brief and the common brief named "`api/_surface.js`'s ROOM_CARD rail" as something to localize; it is Meera's own MULTIPARTY.md group-chat disclosure card, unreachable by any Vyakti Rooms follower, and was left untouched. NOT PROVEN: migration 087 has never executed against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; four statements listed in the final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `vy_room_follower.locale`/`vy_room.default_locale` row has ever been written outside a fake `db`; Devanagari GLYPH rendering was not verified in this sandbox (zero Devanagari-capable fonts installed here per `fc-list`; the layout gate measures geometry, not glyph legibility); the studio's new "Room language" card has no offline eval of its own; no real Telegram update has ever reached this workstream's `/hindi`/`/english` commands. - **WS-R27, forget receipts + the export completeness battery (migration 090)** — a follower's "forget me in this room" used to return counts and then nothing they could keep; now it also writes `vy_room_forget_receipt` (receipt_id, room_id, `person_hash` — a plain SHA-256 of room_id+person_id+policy_version, no `person_id` column at all — policy_version, `counts` jsonb, issued_at), shown once on the Room's "gone" screen with a "Save receipt" JSON download and never surfaced anywhere else, since law 3 is that nothing exists to look it up by later (`decisions.md#ws-r27-forget-receipt-hash-recomputed-not-looked-up`: a PLAIN hash, not the HMAC `api/_replica-full-erasure.js`'s own deletion receipt uses, because nobody but the follower who just received it ever looks one up; the account-wide whole wipe deletes a person's own past receipts by reading the small receipt table and RECOMPUTING the same hash against the wiping person, no new lookup index or env var needed). Second half of the brief: nine Room-scoped person-lane tables had landed since WS-R1 (day counts, checkins + their delivery ledger, voice usage, subscriptions, Pulse opt-ins, the Telegram pointer, push subscriptions, handoff) and nothing had ever proven `roomExport` named all of them or `roomForget` cleared all of them — `evals/room-export/run.mjs` (the 16th named release gate) now does, static (a DDL scan of every `PERSON_TABLES` entry carrying both `room_id` and `person_id` against a new `roomExportManifest()`) plus dynamic (one real follower through the real follower lane touching every surface; export carries a row or count from each; forget leaves zero; the receipt's counts equal what was deleted), with two negative controls that must fail and do (`measurements.md#ws-r27-room-export-completeness-battery-2026-09-04`, 33/33). Building it found a real, previously-invisible bug and fixed it: `roomForget` deleted `vy_room_thread`/`vy_room_follower` FIRST, so every child table's own explicit delete ran AFTER Postgres's own cascade had already removed the rows, making four of those counts unconditionally zero forever — `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES` array had the identical ordering bug for the account-wide whole wipe. Both are now ordered child-before-parent (`rejected.md#ws-r27-child-before-parent-ordering-bug-in-roomforget-and-persontables`), and three tables previously reached ONLY by cascade (`vy_room_subscription`, `vy_room_follower_channel`, `vy_room_push_subscription`) now have their own named, counted delete. One discovered gap left deliberately unfixed and written down rather than patched over-scope: `vy_room_subscription.follower_id`'s `on delete cascade` still removes a LIVE subscription's row the moment a follower's membership row goes, regardless of the `wipeWhere` restriction either wipe's own statement now honours — an owner-level payment-safety schema decision this workstream's brief did not ask for (`decisions.md#ws-r27-subscription-cascade-still-reaches-a-live-row`). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **16/16 without `NEON_URL`** (up from 15 — the new `room export completeness` gate), reconfirmed on the fully modified tree after one documented `EADDRINUSE:8931` collision from a concurrent sibling worktree resolved by rerunning. `node evals/run.mjs` (every registered suite): 0 failures. `node evals/run.mjs sqlcast` caught one real uncast bound parameter on first write (migration 090's `policy_version`), fixed before commit. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean. NOT PROVEN: no statement this workstream wrote has ever run against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment); no real `vy_room_forget_receipt` row exists outside a fake `db`; no human has opened the Room's "gone" screen with a receipt on it.

- **WS-R26, abuse limits on the public doors (2026-09-04)** — migration 089 adds `vy_public_rate` (scope, key_hash, window_start, count; no person/device/owner column, checked against `evals/persontables.mjs`'s `PERSON_COLUMNS` before writing the migration header, no new exemption needed). `api/_rate-limit.js` holds every decision: `consume()` is one upsert whose `on conflict ... do update ... where count < $limit returning count` IS the whole predicate (`ws-r26-write-is-the-check-not-read-then-write`), never a read then a separate write; `hashKey()` is sha256 of the scope, the caller's raw key, `RATE_SALT` (or a fixed fallback), and the UTC day (`ws-r26-key-is-hashed-with-a-daily-salt`); `limitsFor()` merges `DEFAULT_LIMITS` with an operator's `RATE_LIMITS_JSON`, an unknown scope refusing rather than minting a new ceiling (`ws-r26-limits-are-code-constants-not-a-database-table`); `purgeStalePublicRateWindows()` deletes windows older than a day, called best-effort inside `api/_checkins.js`'s existing 15-minute sweep. Wired at seven doors: `api/room.js`'s `open`/`join` (IP-keyed, before a follower row exists), `say` (a 30/min burst limit above the monthly cap, keyed by the follower's own person id off the already-HMAC-verified session) and `push_subscribe` (follower-keyed); `api/apply.js`'s `submit`; `api/room-tg.js` and `api/_payments.js`'s `applyWebhook`, both strictly AFTER their existing auth checks (law #5) — which meant rewriting `api/room-tg.js`'s own header comment, since it previously stated an unqualified "always 200 once authenticated" that a rate-limit 429 now has one deliberate exception to (`ws-r26-webhook-429-is-a-second-exit-from-always-200`). Front end: `src/room/roomApi.ts`'s `RoomApiError` carries `retryAfterSeconds`; `src/room/copy.ts` gets one honest string (`errors.rateLimited`, "Too many attempts from this connection. Try again in N minutes.") and a `withRetry` pluralizing helper; `RoomApp.tsx`'s `send()` hands a rate-limited draft back to the follower exactly as the free-cap branch already does. `evals/rate-limit/run.mjs`, 63/63 offline, three negative controls (unknown scope refused before any write; five unsigned webhook attempts write zero rows while a correctly-signed one over its own limit is refused by name, proven through the REAL `applyWebhook` and the fake payment provider; two IPs never share a counter), plus a static proof that every wired door really calls through the module at the required position. `verify-release.mjs` 15/15 without `NEON_URL` before and after (baseline reconfirmed clean first); `check-copy.mjs` and `context.mjs --check` both clean. Not measured: no real `vy_public_rate` row has ever been written outside a fake `db` (no `NEON_URL` in this environment), and every limit number is a stated judgment call, not a measured traffic ceiling — no real Room traffic exists yet to measure against (`context/rejected.md#ws-r26-static-order-proof-indexof-matched-the-definition-not-the-call` records a same-session fixture lesson: a static "runs before" order-proof must search for a call SITE, never a bare function name that is also how the file spells its own definition).

Open items from this wave: the OTP sign-in doors are not behind the rate table (`ws-r26-otp-doors-not-behind-vy-public-rate`); the whole-wipe receipt read is capped at 10,000 rows (`ws-r27-whole-wipe-receipt-read-capped-at-10000`); every limit in `DEFAULT_LIMITS` is a judgment until real traffic exists. Wave six is complete: migrations 087 to 090 are live; 091 is next.
- **WS-R31, the studio collapsed to Feed/Meet/Share (no migration)** — thirty-odd panels and a wizard rail now sit behind three tabs a creator can hold in their head. `src/studio/StudioShell.tsx` is a NEW presentation layer only: it renders the exact same `ReplicaWorkspace` (exported from `StudioApp.tsx` for the first time, otherwise byte-identical) the old wizard rail already rendered, with the same `step` prop, so no panel, API or blocker moved or forked (`decisions.md#ws-r31-tabs-are-a-new-presentation-over-the-same-panel-tree-not-a-fork`). Above it, a new headline block: FEED reads source count and processing state (`wizardInput.platformWork`, already fetched); MEET reads Readiness's own number and suggested action plus the interview's next topic (`ReadinessPanel`/`MirrorCallStudio` gained additive `onReadiness`/`onInterviewPreview` callbacks); SHARE reads the Room's draft/published/paused state, follower count and link (`RoomStudio` gained an additive `onRoomState` callback, and `firstRoomBlocker()` moved into `roomPublishApi.ts` so it stays bundleable offline). Each tab's ONE primary control is `wizard.steps[i].top` — the wizard's own "next thing," never a second lookup against `QuickStartPath`'s `BLOCKER_META` (imported, not copied, for the Meet tab's re-homed "still locked" breakdown only; `decisions.md#ws-r31-primary-control-routes-through-wizards-top-not-a-second-blocker-meta-lookup`). A plain "All panels" link (`StudioApp.tsx`'s `showAllPanels` state, a runtime toggle, never a rebuild) returns to the old rail. `VITE_STUDIO_SHELL` is the one new env var, and it defaults ON — the one flag in this repo that does (`decisions.md#ws-r31-studio-shell-unset-is-on`; `docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md` §26). `evals/studio-shell/run.mjs` (64/64, bundles the real `studioShellModel.ts` with esbuild): an orphan check that every existing panel is reachable from the shell or the old view (caught `CheckinsCard`/`HandoffCard` as legitimately nested inside `RoomStudio` rather than orphaned, and `main.tsx` as a false positive from a bare `.tsx` filter — both fixed in the check, not the source), the headline/primary-control properties per tab, and three negative controls (a struck import IS caught orphaned; a two-element `primary` array IS refused; a `"train"`/`"model"` string DOES fail `check-copy.mjs` — the first draft of that last control false-passed on a bare `const s = "..."` fixture invisible to the scanner's own visibility heuristic, `rejected.md#ws-r31-a-bare-string-literal-is-invisible-to-check-copy`). `check-layout.mjs` gained a `studio:shell` target scoped to the shell's own new elements, at the gate's existing three viewports. Gate: 16/16 on the untouched tree; 15/16 on first pass after this workstream's own changes (18 contrast findings, one CSS mistake — `--ink-faint` on `--paper`, 3.67:1, and `--state-waiting` on `--paper` at body size, 4.37:1, both under DESIGN-LAW §3's 4.5:1 floor — repeated across four rules); 16/16 confirmed after the fix (`measurements.md#ws-r31-gate-results-2026-09-04`). No new SQL, no new server-side env var. NOT PROVEN: no human has opened a build with the shell on in a real browser; the eval's fixtures are representative, not an exhaustive property sweep the way `evals/studiowizard.mjs` covers `wizardModel.ts`.

**2026-09-04, WS-R31 merged (the studio collapsed to Feed, Meet, Share).** First of wave seven, merged over the wave-six tip plus the docs correction; the branch was a pure descendant, so no conflicts and the context files appended as-is (graph +6 nodes, +8 edges). - **2026-09-04, WS-R28, Suites v0 (migration 091)** - the B2B unit: an organisation owns seats, never a follower. Rooms sells to a creator (B2C); this workstream builds the mechanism a later phase reuses to sell the same product to an institute, a gym, a clinic - a Suite pays for seats, each seat is one creator's Room, and the organisation sees what a creator sees, only as counts. `vy_org` (`created_by_user_id`, deliberately NOT `owner_user_id` - see `context/decisions.md#ws-r28-vy-org-creator-column-is-not-named-owner-user-id`, the org must survive its last admin's own erasure and relcheck's owner-lane walk is a blunt text search for a `delete from vy_org` statement this table must never carry), `vy_org_member` (`owner_user_id`, `role` in `admin`/`creator`, the OWNER lane proper - deleted by name in `api/_replica-full-erasure.js`), `vy_org_subscription` (the Suite's own per-seat money ledger, `api/_payments.js`'s twin-provider seam restated, Phase 0 shape - table and read only, no start-subscription writer built, matching `_payments.js`'s own original scope). `vy_room` gains a nullable `org_id`, `on delete set null` rather than the brief's literal "cascade" (`context/decisions.md#ws-r28-room-org-fk-on-delete-set-null-not-cascade` - a Suite's own deletion must never silently take a Room, its followers and its revenue with it). `api/_org.js` holds every decision: `createOrg` (atomic admin-membership CTE), `inviteMember` (NO write - v0 has no email lookup, so an admin can only hand out the Suite's own id; `acceptMembership` is the only writer of anyone's membership row for themselves, always their own `owner_user_id` - `context/decisions.md#ws-r28-suite-membership-is-always-the-members-own-write`), `attachRoom` (law 2: one UPDATE carrying admin-exists, seat-count-under-limit and creator-accepted all in its own WHERE clause, zero rows refused with a named reason - `not_admin`/`no_seat`/`creator_not_member` plus `room_not_found`/`room_already_attached` - read back from a diagnostic select only AFTER the zero-row write, `api/_room-publish.js`'s publish-lock shape exactly), `detachRoom` (room's own owner OR an org admin, self-service either way), `orgBoard` (law 3: admin-only, 404 by name for a non-member, reuses `api/_ops.js`'s own `roomOverview` - now exported - per Room rather than re-deriving the same aggregate query), `orgSubscriptionStatus`/`listMyOrgs`/`listOrgMembers`/`roomSuiteStatus`, and `seatCoversCreatorTier` (law 4's exemption predicate, built and proven even though no creator tier charge exists anywhere in this codebase yet to consult it - `context/decisions.md#ws-r28-seat-covers-creator-tier-predicate-built-unwired`). `api/org.js` is the thin owner-authenticated door, `api/replica.js`'s own bearer shape. Front end: `src/studio/orgApi.ts` + `SuiteCard.tsx` (create a Suite, seats used/limit, invite/join by pasted id, members, attach/detach this Room), mounted in `RoomStudio.tsx` alongside `CheckinsCard`; the Room's own address card gains a one-line "Part of `<Suite name>`" state, fed by the same `roomSuite` read `SuiteCard` uses so the two can never disagree. `evals/org/run.mjs` (new suite, registered in `evals/run.mjs`): 54/54 offline, three required negative controls all proven to fire (a non-admin attach writes nothing; the leak battery's own aggregate-only parser, copied inline, catches a follower-leaking select list; a Room attached to org A is invisible to org B's board in both directions), plus a static §8 confirming the erasure job deletes the membership row by name and never the Suite itself. One real bug caught before commit and logged (`context/rejected.md#ws-r28-leak-battery-scanner-matches-prose-not-only-sql`): naming the two follower/thread tables in `api/_org.js`'s own header PROSE tripped the leak battery's blunt whole-file substring scanner, which then demanded a real SQL statement that did not exist; fixed by paraphrasing around the literal names. A second, more serious bug was caught by `node --check` before the gate ever ran: this workstream's first edit to `api/_replica-full-erasure.js` used markdown-style backticks (`` `vy_org` ``) inside a `-- comment` that lives INSIDE that file's giant SQL template literal, terminating the JS string early and producing a real syntax error - fixed by removing the backticks from that one comment; `evals/replica-erasure/run.mjs` (20/20) and the full gate both confirmed clean afterward. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **14/16 on the untouched tree** (2 environmental failures reproduced with zero Suites files present - "eval suite" reporting a stale `api/_engine.gen.js`, and "room leak battery" at 77/78 with the specific failing assertion lost to `tail`-truncation and never reproduced across four later standalone runs, logged open rather than claimed fixed: `context/measurements.md#ws-r28-room-leak-baseline-single-failure-unexplained-2026-09-04`) and **16/16 after** this workstream's full tree, `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` itself at 78/78. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls, unchanged. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 967 nodes, 1190 edges. `node evals/persontables.mjs`: 129 person-keyed tables, 72 owner-lane (up from 71 - `vy_org_member` joins it; `vy_org` itself never appears in this scan by design, since `created_by_user_id` is not a `PERSON_COLUMNS` name). `node evals/sqlcast.mjs`: 160 tables (+3), 0 conflicts, 0 uncast sites. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: migration 091 has never executed against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; 34 statements from `db/migrations/apply.mjs`'s own `splitSql`, and every real SQL statement in `api/_org.js` and the erasure addition, listed verbatim in this workstream's final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `vy_org`/`vy_org_member`/`vy_org_subscription` row exists outside a fake `db`; `scripts/relcheck.mjs` did not run (no reachable database in this environment); no human has ever seen `SuiteCard.tsx` render in a browser; the GroupAI kernel port (cross-Room disclosure) is explicitly out of scope and untouched. Did not push; on branch `ws-r28-suites`.

**2026-09-04, WS-R29 (check-ins over WhatsApp utility templates, migration 092), built.** Wires the seam WS-R16 named but explicitly left unsent ("out of scope to SEND") onto `api/whatsapp.js`'s existing Cloud API precedent: `vy_room_follower_whatsapp` (a follower's own opt-in phone, PRIMARY KEY `follower_id`, PERSON lane) plus `api/_room-whatsapp.js` (opt-in/stop/status, the pure scannable `buildTemplatePayload`, `sendTemplate`, and the webhook — `verifyRoomWhatsappWebhook` is `api/whatsapp.js`'s own `verify()` reused verbatim, never a second HMAC implementation) plus `api/room-wa.js` (the thin HTTP door: HMAC, then WS-R26's `room_wa_ip` rate scope, then the status write). `api/_checkins.js`'s `deliverers.whatsappTemplate` now actually sends: a TEMPLATE always (`vyakti_checkin_v1`, constants for name and language, never a request field or an env var), gated structurally absent behind `ROOM_WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_APPROVED` (unset means the channel never appears in a follower's choices, `INVITES_REQUIRED`'s own shape), a 4xx revokes the opt-in and stops further sends EXCEPT 429 (deliberately excluded — see `rejected.md#ws-r29-429-treated-as-a-generic-4xx-would-have-revoked-a-good-number`, a real bug this workstream's own tests caught and fixed before it shipped), a 429/5xx writes no ledger row at all. Inbound replies get exactly one deterministic, app-voiced line (never a model call — `surface-bypasses-parse` binds here too) reused through `api/whatsapp.js`'s own `send`/`noteInbound`, never persisting a follower's words; a second real bug was caught the same way (`rejected.md#ws-r29-meta-wire-phone-format-vs-stored-e164-mismatch`, Meta's digits-only wire phone versus the stored E.164 "+" format). Export/forget: `roomExport` carries a count, a state and a MASKED number for this table (never the digits in full), `roomForget` deletes it by name, `PERSON_TABLES` and `evals/recall/run.mjs`'s own FATE table both carry a written entry. Ops board: a new platform-wide (never per-room) `whatsapp: {template_sends_this_month, cost_this_month_inr}` field, the unit cost (₹0.11, a comment cites the source) a named constant imported rather than restated. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **16/16 on the untouched tree** (baseline, before any change) and **16/16 after**, both without `NEON_URL`. `node evals/run.mjs`: exit 0, every suite including the new `room-whatsapp` (63/63, `measurements.md#ws-r29-room-whatsapp-offline-battery-2026-09-04`). Building this workstream found and fixed one real regression in code it did not otherwise touch — wiring the WhatsApp deliverer into the sweep's own `deliverOne` meant several PRE-EXISTING `evals/checkins/run.mjs` assertions that counted ledger rows without filtering by `channel` started over-counting; fixed by scoping them. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls bit. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 967 nodes, 1191 edges. New env vars: `ROOM_WHATSAPP_TEMPLATE_APPROVED` (required, structural gate), `ROOM_WHATSAPP_LINK_ORIGIN` (optional, cosmetic — the domain prefix on the link a template's body carries; unset degrades to a bare `/r/<slug>` path). No `ROOM_WHATSAPP_*` credential vars were invented — the existing `WHATSAPP_ACCESS_TOKEN`/`WHATSAPP_PHONE_NUMBER_ID`/`WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET`/`WHATSAPP_VERIFY_TOKEN` are reused, an explicit workstream instruction and this session's own decision (`decisions.md#ws-r29-whatsapp-credentials-reused-not-forked`), which also names the one thing this workstream could NOT resolve: whether Meta's webhook subscription model will let this file (`api/room-wa.js`) and `api/whatsapp.js` share one WABA number's callback URL, unproven either way in an environment with no real WhatsApp credentials. NOT PROVEN, named rather than implied: no statement in migration 092 has ever run against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` here); no real `vy_room_follower_whatsapp` row exists outside a fake `db`; no real Meta webhook has ever reached `api/room-wa.js`; no human has seen the "Check-ins on WhatsApp" control in a real browser. Migration 092 applied nowhere live yet — the main loop's own job, per every prior workstream's own division of labour.

- **WS-R30, the conversion moment (2026-09-04)** — the plan's Phase 2 gate needs three numbers (paid conversion >=12%, week-six retention >=35%, three creators renewing unasked); before this workstream nothing measured conversion at all, since `vy_room_subscription` only ever gets a row once a follower already said yes, and the only upgrade prompt a follower ever saw was the capped card, shown only after refusal. Migration 093 adds `vy_room_upgrade_offer` (offer_id, room_id, person_id, follower_id, shown_at, reason in ('session_worked','cap_reached'), outcome/outcome_at, both null or both set together; index on `(follower_id, shown_at desc)`; PERSON lane, `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES` and `REPLICA_PERSON_TABLES`, `roomExport`/`roomForget`'s `ROOM_EXPORT_EXTRA`, `evals/recall/run.mjs`'s FATE table as "forget-only"). `api/_phase-gate.js` holds every decision: `sessionWorked` (law 1's predicate — free tier, >=4 of the follower's own messages in the current 30-minute-gap session read from `meera_log` (never `vy_room_follower_day`, which cannot resolve a 30-minute boundary), the thread continued from an earlier calendar day, and either <5 messages remaining this month or a prior CALENDAR MONTH's summed turns already reached the room's cap — one SQL statement, six CTEs, the `vy_room_follower`-touching CTE placed FIRST so `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s own first-select-from parser judges the correct aggregate-only segment, `ws-r30-session-worked-one-statement-follower-scope-first`); `recordOffer` (INSERT...WHERE NOT EXISTS, the 14-day cooldown IS the write, `vy_public_rate`'s own upsert-is-the-check shape one file over); `markOfferOutcome` (the follower's own most recent OPEN offer only, never a client-supplied offer id); `conversionReport` (per room, aggregate-only, eligible = joined 14-60 days ago, paying/eligible, plus the offer funnel by reason); `renewedUnasked` (an honest, hardcoded zero — `api/_payments.js`'s own header already says a creator-tier subscription table is "a Phase 2 concern with no table here", so nothing can be measured, and the note says so on the card rather than a fabricated number); `phaseGate` (loops every room exactly as `api/_ops.js`/`api/_funnel.js` already do — "never a grouped statement across rooms" — composing `below`/`at_or_above`/`not_enough_data` per number plus one sentence, thresholds imported from `api/_room-cohorts.js`'s existing `PAID_CONVERSION_FLOOR_PCT`/`PHASE2_FLOOR_PCT` rather than re-typed). `api/_room-surface.js`'s `roomSay` gains `offer: {reason, price_inr, currency}` on the response, computed AFTER the reply already left through `gatedReply` (byte-identical reply text with or without an offer, proven as a negative control) and only for a free follower with a real thread; the cap-reached refusal branch also best-effort records a `cap_reached` offer before throwing, so the refusal itself is unchanged; `roomDismissOffer` (a new self-scoped op, no offer id trusted from the client) backs "Continue free". `api/_payments.js`'s `applyWebhook` inlines a fifth CTE (`offer_update`, spliced in only when migration 093 has landed) marking the follower's most recent open offer 'paid' in the SAME statement as the subscription's own state flip — never a second round trip, never a call to `markOfferOutcome` itself, since that function must also serve the standalone dismiss path (`ws-r30-webhook-offer-update-inlined-not-called`). `api/_ops.js`/`OpsBoard.tsx` gain a Phase gate card (three numbers, badges, the offer funnel table, the one sentence). `src/room/copy.ts` gains an `offer` block in both locales; `RoomApp.tsx` renders it alongside (never replacing) the existing quota nudge. `evals/phase-gate/run.mjs`: 49/49 offline, real turns through the real follower lane and the real payments webhook, three negative controls (a message-body-shaped select caught by room-leak's own aggregate-only parser copied inline; a second offer inside 14 days never inserts; the reply is byte-identical with and without an offer). Two real defects were found and fixed by the gates themselves before any count was recorded: `_phase-gate.js` had to be added to `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s `AGGREGATE_ONLY` set BY NAME even though its SQL was already shaped correctly (`ws-r30-phase-gate-not-registered-in-leak-battery`); `evals/sqlcast`'s strict-surface rule caught one real uncast `outcome_at = $3` the moment the file was added to `STRICT_SURFACE`, fixed to `($3)::timestamptz`. A separate eval-fixture lesson, worth a future session's five minutes: a third invented auth id in a test fell through `evals/room/fixtures.mjs`'s `USER_A`/`USER_B`-only clean-hex mapping into a `pp<slice>` shape that correctly failed strict UUID validation (`ws-r30-third-synthetic-user-id-fails-strict-uuid-validation`). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **16/16 on the untouched tree, confirmed first via a WIP-commit-and-`git reset --hard`/`--soft` round trip, and 16/16 again after every file in this report** (`measurements.md#ws-r30-phase-gate-offline-eval-2026-09-04`). `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls, unchanged. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 970 nodes / 1192 edges. A process note logged rather than hidden: this session issued `git stash` once by accident mid-verification, against the explicit cross-worktree prohibition, and reversed it in the same turn with `git stash pop` before any other command ran, confirming every file intact afterward (`ws-r30-git-stash-run-once-by-accident-mid-session`) — no evidence of any collision, but the rule is restated rather than treated as merely reconfirmed. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: no statement in migration 093 or `api/_phase-gate.js` has ever run against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; every new statement is listed verbatim in this workstream's final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `vy_room_upgrade_offer` row exists outside a fake `db`; no real follower has ever seen the offer card; the Phase gate card's three numbers have never been read off a live database. Did not push; on branch `ws-r30-conversion`. No env vars added.

**2026-09-04, WS-R32 (hardening: the OTP doors, and the whole-wipe receipt
sweep bounded by Rooms, migration 094), built.** Closes the two open items
logged at the wave-six merges. (1) `api/account.js`'s `send_sms`/`verify_sms`
- the OTP sign-in the Room uses - now consume from `vy_public_rate` (WS-R26)
at `otp_send_ip`/`otp_send_dest`/`otp_verify_ip`/`otp_verify_dest`
(`api/_rate-limit.js`'s `DEFAULT_LIMITS`: 10/hr + 30/hr for send, 10/min +
30/hr for verify), on top of the existing in-memory `otp_dest` throttle,
which stays as a fast first layer and is explicitly documented as not enough
alone (per-instance, resets on cold start); `verify_sms` had NO throttle of
any kind before this, so `otp_verify_dest`'s 10-a-minute ceiling is now the
actual brute-force floor against a 6-digit code, not a supplement to one.
Both doors validate the destination BEFORE gating, so a malformed
destination never touches the counter. (2) The account-wide whole wipe's
receipt sweep (`api/memory.js`) is rewritten as an extracted, injectable
function, `purgeRoomForgetReceipts(db, personId)`: it walks every `vy_room`
row (no `limit`, bounded by Rooms - hundreds at most in Phase 1 - rather
than by receipts) times every `ROOM_FORGET_RECEIPT_POLICY_VERSION`,
recomputes each candidate `roomForgetReceiptHash`, and deletes
`vy_room_forget_receipt where person_hash = any($1)` in one statement under
migration 094's new index on `person_hash` - replacing the old `select ...
limit 10000` read, which silently stopped reaching older receipts once the
table passed that size and could never have reached a person whose Room A
follower row was already gone (they forgot it earlier) by walking "the
rooms this person currently follows" instead. Extracted specifically so a
test could drive it through a fake `db` at all - `purgeRelational` itself
calls `q` directly with no injection seam anywhere else in this file.
`evals/rate-limit/run.mjs`: 80/80 (up from 63) - the four scopes, an
11-attempt brute-force negative control through the REAL `consume()`, and a
static proof (call-site literals, not bare names -
`ws-r26-static-order-proof-indexof-matched-the-definition-not-the-call`'s
own discipline) that validation precedes both gates. `evals/room-export/run.mjs`:
44/44 (up from 33, still the 16th named release gate) - a new layer 4
proves the exact scenario the fix targets: forget Room A, join Room B (a
second room seeded directly into the fixture, reusing the one demo sheet),
whole-wipe, Room A's receipt is gone though no follower row names it any
more, and a stray receipt hashed for a different person survives. One
rejection logged on the way: the new static "the old read is gone" check
first failed on the FIXED tree because `purgeRoomForgetReceipts`'s own
header comment quoted the old SQL shape it was explaining, and the regex
matched the comment rather than the code
(`ws-r32-static-check-matched-its-own-explanatory-comment`) - fixed by
rewording the comment to prose. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **16/16
on the untouched tree** (confirmed via a separate detached git worktree at
7729450 rather than `git stash`, removed after the read) **and 16/16
after**, both without `NEON_URL`. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes
clean, 21 negative controls. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean,
1002 nodes / 1233 edges. Two decisions logged with reversal conditions
(`ws-r32-otp-doors-behind-vy-public-rate`, `ws-r32-whole-wipe-receipt-sweep-bounded-by-rooms`),
each with a `supersedes` edge onto the open node it closes; one measurement;
one rejection. No new env vars. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: neither the four
`consume()` calls in `api/account.js` nor `purgeRoomForgetReceipts`'s two
statements have ever run against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this
environment - every new SQL statement is listed verbatim in this
workstream's final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real
`vy_public_rate` row has ever been written by an OTP door outside a fake
`db`; no real `vy_room_forget_receipt` row has ever been deleted by the new
sweep outside a fake `db`; every OTP limit is a stated judgment call, not a
measured traffic ceiling. Did not push; on branch `ws-r32-hardening`.
Migration 094 is this workstream's; 095 is next.

**2026-09-04, WS-R32 merged (the two logged open items closed).** First of wave eight, merged over the wave-seven tip plus two doc commits; a pure descendant, so no conflicts (graph +4 nodes, +5 edges from the branch, +1 measurement node at the merge). The OTP doors (`send_sms`, `verify_sms`) now consume from `vy_public_rate` under four named scopes by IP and by destination hash, with a 10-a-minute verify floor computed for a six-digit code, the in-memory throttle kept as a faster first layer, and the phone validated before either gate so a malformed destination never touches a counter. The whole wipe's receipt step walks every Room times every policy version, computes the hash, and deletes by `person_hash = any(...)` under the new index from migration 094; the capped read is gone, proven by a scenario where a person forgets Room A, joins Room B, wipes their account, and Room A's receipt is proven gone with no follower row linking them. Both open nodes carry `supersedes` edges from the new decisions and stay in the graph. Migration 094 applied live and read back; three statements EXPLAINed. Gate 16/16 on the merged tree; rate-limit 80/80 (was 63), room-export 44/44 (was 33, and it is a release gate), room 54/54, room-leak 78/78, checkins 37/37, recall 266. One rejection logged: a static check in the new eval matched its own explanatory comment quoting the old SQL, fixed by rewording the comment to prose. Still building: R33 (095), R34 (096), R35 (097).

**2026-09-04, WS-R34 (check-ins over Telegram, migration 096), built.** The fourth check-in channel: WS-R18's one-Room-per-chat pointer (`vy_room_follower_channel`, migration 082) IS the opt-in, no new person table. Migration 096: the `vy_room_checkin_delivery` channel CHECK widened to admit `'telegram'` (dropped and re-added by the SAME name migration 085 already confirmed live, `vy_room_checkin_delivery_channel_check`); `vy_room_follower_channel` gains `checkins_enabled boolean not null default true` (a Telegram Room join is already a two-question gate, so the pointer defaults reachable — `decisions.md#ws-r34-checkins-enabled-default-true-the-pointer-is-the-opt-in`) and `stopped_code text null` (nullable so it doubles as the sendable predicate without a second boolean, `decisions.md#ws-r34-stopped-code-nullable-text-not-a-second-boolean`). `api/_room-surface.js` gains four `follower_id`-scoped functions (`activeTelegramChannelFor`/`markTelegramChannelStopped`/`telegramCheckinsStatusFor`/`setTelegramCheckinsEnabledForFollower`) — the raw table SQL stays in this file per its own convention, so `api/_room-telegram.js` and `api/_checkins.js` never grow a query the leak battery's scanner would need to allowlist. `api/_room-telegram.js` gains `/checkins on|off` (`parseCheckinsCommand`, wired into `handleRoomCommand` before the no-argument command table), `sendRoomCheckinMessage` (a detailed send exposing HTTP status and Telegram's own `retry_after`, deliberately separate from the bot's own reply-wire `tgCall`), and `resolveReplyThreadId` — law 5's reply-to-thread mapping, built as a real injectable seam that ships resolving to `null` (the Room's default thread) every time, because `vy_room_checkin` carries no `thread_id` column and this workstream does not add one (`decisions.md#ws-r34-checkin-thread-mapping-defaults-to-null`). `api/_checkins.js` gains `deliverers.telegram(db, row, said, deps)` — called from `deliverOne` with the SAME `said` the in-app delivery already produced through `gatedReply`, never a second model call (proven by a static source scan finding no `gatedReply`/`think(`/`engine.compile`/`deps.reply` in its own body, plus a behavioural check that the sent text is byte-identical to `said`); states `not_configured`/`skipped_stopped`/`delivered`/`failed`; a 403 or 400 marks the pointer's `stopped_code` and stops further sends (revoke on failure, law 3); a 429 or 5xx writes no ledger row, honouring `retry_after` by logging it rather than acting on it (no per-follower cooldown state exists to act on it with). The Room panel's own control: two new ops on `api/checkins.js` (`telegram_status`/`telegram_set`, session-scoped via the file's existing `followerScope`), wired into `CheckinsPanel.tsx` (a `tgConnected`-gated block, `waAvailable`'s own shape one channel over) with new copy in both locales (`copy.ts`'s `tgTitle`/`tgOnCopy`/`tgOffCopy`/`tgStoppedCopy`/`tgEnable`/`tgDisable`/`tgError`). `evals/room-telegram-checkins/run.mjs`: 64/64 offline, seven sections including two negative controls on the eligibility predicate (checkins_enabled:false, a stopped pointer — neither ever reaches `fetch`) and the one-door proof above; registered in `evals/run.mjs`. One real defect found and fixed by the release gate itself, not by this suite: `evals/sqlcast.mjs` caught a boolean `$2` parameter reused inside a bare `CASE WHEN` without its own cast, reported twice for the same statement (`rejected.md#ws-r34-boolean-parameter-reused-in-a-case-expression-without-a-cast`) — fixed with `($2)::bool` at both sites. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **15/16 on the first full run this session made** (the sqlcast failure above; no separate untouched-tree baseline was captured before this session's edits began, the same honest gap WS-R18 and WS-R22 both logged) and **16/16 after the fix**. Regression-checked unchanged by direct runs: `checkins` 37/37, `room-telegram` 51/51, `room-leak` 78/78, `recall` 266, `persontables` 56 entries, `room-whatsapp` 63/63, `room-push` 43/43, `room` 54/54, `room-export` ok, `room-locale` 44/44. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 1004 nodes, 1235 edges. New env vars: none — `ROOM_TELEGRAM_BOT_TOKEN` is reused, not invented. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: migration 096 has never executed against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; two statements listed in the final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `checkins_enabled`/`stopped_code` value exists outside a fake `db`; no real check-in has ever reached Telegram; no human has seen the "Check-ins on Telegram" control in a real browser. Did not push; on branch `ws-r34-telegram-checkins`.

**2026-09-04, WS-R34 merged (check-ins over Telegram).** Second of wave eight, merged over the WS-R32 tip. Conflicts only in the context files (unioned from the wave-seven base, 0 duplicated headings), the graph (+6 nodes, +7 edges) and the schema (094 then 096, both kept). The Room pointer that already existed is the opt-in: `checkins_enabled` defaults true and `stopped_code` null means sendable, both predicates in the sweep's own SQL; `/checkins on` and `/checkins off` on Telegram and a toggle in the Room panel in both locales; the Telegram delivery carries the same reply the in-app delivery produced through the one door, proven byte-identical; a 403 or 400 marks the pointer stopped, a 429 or 5xx writes no row and honours `retry_after`. The reply-to-thread mapping is a real seam that resolves to null today because check-ins carry no thread id (decision with reversal condition).
