**2026-09-05, WS-R70 built (the creator's export, no migration), on worktree branch `ws-r70-the-creators-export` over a414c7c, not merged, not pushed.** A follower can export and forget (WS-R1/WS-R27); a creator, who gave the platform their archive, their voice and their money, had full erasure (`api/_replica-full-erasure.js`) but no export. New `api/_creator-export.js`: `creatorExport(db, ownerUserId)` walks a hand-maintained `OWNER_LANE_TABLES` manifest (51 tables, seven WHERE shapes) built to the SAME completeness discipline `PERSON_TABLES` already has — `evals/creator-export/run.mjs`'s own static layer parses `api/_replica-full-erasure.js`'s real source, computes the owner-lane subset of what it reaches from the checked-in DDL, and asserts the two lists match exactly. **The one hard lesson this workstream found**: classifying a table as owner-lane by WHICH BLOCK of the erasure file's own SQL it sits in (`agent_id` vs `replica_id`/`owner_user_id`/a `room_id` subquery) is the wrong rule — `vy_room_subscription` is reached from the identical owner-lane-looking SQL position as `vy_payment_event`/`vy_room_price`, but it is a follower's own subscription record; the fix is classifying by table NAME against `api/memory.js`'s `PERSON_TABLES`, never by the erasure file's own scoping predicate (`rejected.md#ws-r70-owner-lane-classification-by-erasure-sql-position-would-have-leaked-a-follower`). `vy_room_thread`/`vy_room_follower` are excluded the same way; `vy_room_handoff` is excluded ENTIRELY (a follower's ask and the creator's own reply share one row with no column split, `decisions.md#ws-r70-creator-export-excludes-vy-room-handoff`); `vy_payment_event` and three erasure-process bookkeeping tables are named, deliberate gaps (`OWNER_LANE_DELIBERATE_GAPS`). New op `export` on `api/replica.js`, rate-limited one a day per owner through a new `creator_export_owner` scope in `api/_rate-limit.js`; `evals/room-doors/run.mjs`'s own OP_COVERAGE gained the entry (excluded from the attack classes on the same grounds `revoke`'s siblings already establish: no cross-identity input, `ownerUserId` comes only from the verified session). A "Download everything" control was added to the studio's account surface (`StudioApp.tsx`, next to Revoke), genuinely bilingual from the start through a new `creatorExport` section in `src/studio/copy.ts` and `useStudioLocale()`, even though the surrounding legacy section stays the pre-existing English-only Tier 2 scope cut. **A second real finding, found only by running the gate**: `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s repo-wide static scans fail the build for ANY `api/*.js` file that so much as MENTIONS `vy_room_thread`/`vy_room_follower`/the Handoff table by name (comment or code alike), and hold every reader of `vy_room_arrival` outside two named files to a stricter "aggregate-only, never `select *`" rule than this export's generic per-table shape could satisfy — fixed by paraphrasing every explanatory comment in prose (never quoting the literal identifier) and dropping `vy_room_arrival` from the manifest entirely rather than fighting an established, unrelated discipline (`rejected.md#ws-r70-mentioning-a-boundary-tables-name-in-a-comment-trips-a-repo-wide-static-scanner`, `decisions.md#ws-r70-room-arrival-excluded-generic-select-conflicts-with-a-sibling-gates-discipline`). **A third real finding**: the new control's body paragraph reused `.danger-zone`'s own pre-existing `640px` pixel `max-width` for a sentence roughly three times longer, and `scripts/check-layout.mjs` (after a `vite build` — the gate serves `dist/`, not source, and a CSS edit with no rebuild is invisible to it) measured 116 characters per line in both languages at `/studio` — fixed with the control's own rule using `var(--measure)` (68ch), the character-based cap the rest of the studio already uses for long-form prose, never the pixel number a shorter neighbour happened to get away with (`rejected.md#ws-r70-shared-pixel-max-width-reused-for-a-longer-sentence-than-it-was-ever-measured-against`). Two OTHER gate failures seen mid-session on a heavily loaded shared machine (10+ concurrent sibling `verify-release.mjs` runs) were proven NOT this workstream's: `layout readability` hit `EADDRINUSE` on port 8931 (a sibling holding it, ws-common.md's own named collision), and `performance budgets`/`accessibility` both flagged the STATIC MARKETING LANDING PAGE (`/`, `site:/`) — `git diff --stat a414c7c..HEAD -- site/ studio.html room.html vercel.json` is EMPTY, and the specific accessibility violation (`.onb-sub`/`.onb-honest` color contrast) traces to `src/components/Onboarding.tsx`/`src/styles/onboard.css`, Meera's own onboarding, also untouched by this diff; both cleared on a full standalone rerun once contention eased (TBT on `/` dropped from 307ms to 45ms with no code change). Final tally after the layout fix, each gate run standalone once the studio dist/ was rebuilt: `layout readability` ok (1497 prose blocks, 0 findings); `performance budgets` ok (4/4 targets); `accessibility` still shows the ONE pre-existing, unrelated `site:/` finding (not this workstream's, not fixed here — out of scope); `security headers` ok; `room leak battery` 81/81; `room export completeness` 44/44; `room door battery` 494/494; creator-export 40/40; studio-locale 39/39; `tsc -b` clean; `scripts/check-copy.mjs` clean (6 scopes, 21 negative controls); `node scripts/context.mjs --check` clean (1261 nodes, 1514 edges). No migration: the manifest is a JS constant, not a database table (117 remains the next free number). Not proven: no real owner has ever run a real export against a live database (no `NEON_URL` in this worktree) — every measurement is offline, against a fake `db`, and the seeded-size figure (3,679 bytes for a four-table world) is stated plainly as a lower bound, not a realistic creator's export size; the pre-existing `site:/` accessibility finding was not fixed (out of this workstream's scope) and remains open for whichever workstream owns Meera's onboarding screen.

**2026-09-05, WS-R70 merged (the creator's export). Wave twelve complete.** Tenth of wave twelve. Conflicts in the context files (unioned from a414c7c), the graph (+10 nodes, +10 edges), the rate-limit scopes, the eval registry and three hunks of `src/studio/copy.ts`, where the showcase interface and both showcase objects lost their closers to the both-added export block and were closed again. No migration; 117 stays unused. `api/_creator-export.js` walks a manifest of 52 owner-lane tables in seven scope shapes and returns one JSON document with counts and storage pointers, never bytes and never a follower-lane row; its eval proves the manifest equals the owner-lane subset of the erasure cascade's own reach, and at this merge it caught WS-R62's operator table missing (added) while the world battery caught the export reading renewal reminders without a role (admitted as the creator-slice reader it is). Three deliberate gaps are named with reasons: `vy_room_handoff` (one row holds both parties' words), `vy_payment_event` and the erasure bookkeeping, `vy_room_arrival` (aggregate-only). Once a day per owner; a Download-everything control beside Revoke, both locales. The representative statements were `EXPLAIN`ed live (`measurements.md#ws-r70-creator-export-statements-live-explain-2026-09-05`). creator-export 40/40, leak battery 201/201, door battery 544/544. **Wave twelve: ten workstreams built, ten merged (R64, R62, R68, R65, R69, R67, R61, R63, R66, R70); migrations 114, 115 and 116 live; 117 unused; the gate stays at 21 checks; the door battery is at 544 cases, the leak battery at ten layers.** Open into wave thirteen: the nineteen studio panels still in English and the two consent screens awaiting legal review; the showcase card picker; the NPCI marks behind `npci.org.in`; Razorpay refusing seat-quantity updates on UPI mandates (the Suite lane); both Vercel projects still without env; nothing in this wave exercised by a real person.

**2026-09-05, WS-R78 built (the poster and the QR, migration 121), on worktree branch `ws-r78-poster-and-qr` over 8b154f8, not merged, not pushed.** A creator prints one sheet for a coaching centre's notice board; a follower scans it and lands in the Room. New `api/_qr.js`: a pure, hand-written QR encoder (byte mode, EC level M, versions 1-10, no third-party runtime — `qrcode` (npm) was measured and rejected as a production dependency because it is NOT zero-dependency at the version checked, `decisions.md#ws-r78-own-qr-encoder-not-npm-qrcode`). **Two real, serious bugs shipped in the first draft and were caught only by installing the reference `qrcode` package as a debugging-only oracle (never committed) and pointing a real, independent scanner (`jsqr`, npm, zero dependencies, added as a devDependency) at actual rendered pixels**: a byte-reversed Reed-Solomon generator polynomial (`rejected.md#ws-r78-reversed-rs-generator-polynomial-passed-every-self-check`) and an MSB-first format-info write where the spec wants LSB-first (`rejected.md#ws-r78-format-info-msb-first-was-unscannable`) — BOTH bugs passed every self-referential check this file had (RS divisibility, BCH known-vector tables, a self-consistency round trip) because a self-check can only prove a routine agrees with itself, never that it agrees with the world; `jsqr` failed to decode a plain "HELLO" QR under all 8 candidate masks before either fix, and decoded 10/10 versions correctly after (`measurements.md#ws-r78-qr-encoder-real-scanner-verification-2026-09-05`). `api/_room-card.js` gains a third `kind`, `poster` (A4 at 150dpi, 1240x1754, `ROOM_CARD_SIZES`/`cardInputFor`/`computeCardLayout`/`renderRoomCard`/`rasterizeRoomCard` all extended, the SAME layout function and bundled Devanagari face WS-R55 already ships): the creator's name, the one-line bio, the QR (centred, quiet-zone-correct), the disclosure sentence, and the URL in plain text under the QR for people who cannot scan, both locales by the Room's own default locale. The encoded URL is `https://<origin>/r/<slug>?via=poster`; a paused-or-unknown poster's QR encodes the bare origin instead, never a slug, keeping WS-R40's own "never learn whether a slug exists" law intact for a QR payload the same way it already holds for pixels (`decisions.md#ws-r78-platform-poster-qr-encodes-bare-origin`). `poster` joins `ROOM_ARRIVAL_VIA` AND migration 121 (which widens `vy_room_arrival`'s CHECK to match) in the SAME commit — unlike WS-R59's install/113 split, this workstream's own brief names the law explicitly ("never one without the other"), so there is no asymmetry window this time (`decisions.md#ws-r78-migration-121-ships-with-the-js-allowlist-in-one-commit`). `vercel.json` gains the `/r/:slug/poster.png` rewrite; the Share tab gains a "Download poster" link (`posterLink`, `roomPublishApi.ts`) beside the existing story-card one, its own `poster` section in `copy.ts` (both locales, its own closed block, never inserted into `roomStudio`'s existing one); the ops board's Growth section gains a poster-arrivals line (`posterArrivalsThisWeek`, `api/_funnel.js`, the identical n>=5 floor `shareArrivalsThisWeek` already uses, one `via` value over). New `evals/qr/run.mjs` (60/60): GF(256) hygiene, RS divisibility via an independent polynomial-division routine, BCH known-vector tables (format info for EC-M all 8 masks, version info for 7-10) against the standard published constants, structural sanity across a version spread, a self-consistency round trip, AND — the layer that actually caught the two real bugs — `jsqr` decoding real rendered pixels across versions 1/4/7/8 and four masks, with a negative control that erases a finder pattern's own pixels. `evals/room-card/run.mjs` extended to 66/66: `slug` joins the allowed `row` fields (still one of `publicRoomBySlug`'s own four public columns); a new §6 decodes the poster's QR from the real rasterised PNG via `jsqr` for both locales and the platform card, asserts the plain-text URL matches, and negative-controls a corrupted finder pattern. `evals/room-share/run.mjs` extended to 57/57: the stale "four DB-backed values plus one JS-only" assertion (true only during WS-R59's own asymmetry window) is replaced with a direct cross-check of `ROOM_ARRIVAL_VIA` against migration 121's own CHECK clause text; a new §4b mirrors §4's floor proof for `posterArrivalsThisWeek`, including that a same-day share arrival never leaks into the poster count; the fake `db`'s own `vy_room_arrival` reader was fixed to parse the `via` value out of the real SQL text rather than hardcoding "share" (it would have silently given the poster line the share line's own numbers otherwise). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **20/21**, the one failure (`layout readability`) reproducing as `EADDRINUSE:8931` from a concurrently-running sibling worktree (`ws-r79`, confirmed by PID/cwd), the exact named collision `ws-common.md` describes — not this workstream's. A full standalone `node evals/run.mjs`: exit 0, every one of 27 suites 0 failed (751 total lines of suite output), including `qr` 60/60, `room-card` 66/66, `room-share` 57/57, `room-leak` 201/201 (still ten layers — `poster` is aggregate-only arrival counting, no new person-lane table), `room-doors` 544/544 (unchanged — `room-card.js` stays GET-only, excluded from the door battery on the same rule `og`/`story` already are). `node node_modules/typescript/bin/tsc -b`: clean. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: clean, 6 scopes, 21 negative controls, unchanged. Render cost measured directly (`measurements.md#ws-r78-poster-render-time-and-bytes-2026-09-05`): 333.9ms cold, 162.0ms warm; poster PNG 79,256 bytes. No new env var. Migration 121 is one statement (`drop constraint if exists` + `add constraint`, idempotent), never run against a live Postgres in this worktree (no `NEON_URL`) — listed verbatim in this workstream's final report for the main loop's own `EXPLAIN`. Not proven: no real phone camera has scanned a printed poster (no camera in this sandboxed environment — `jsqr` is a real, independent, actively-maintained decoder, but it is still software); no live Vercel deployment has served `/r/:slug/poster.png`; the layout gate's own room-card block (which would exercise the poster through a real Chromium render alongside `og`/`story`) has not been confirmed standalone this session past the port collision above. Did not push; on branch `ws-r78-poster-and-qr`.

**2026-09-05, WS-R73 built (Suites on UPI), on worktree branch `ws-r73-suites-on-upi` over 8b154f8, not merged, not pushed, no migration.** Closed the wave-twelve open item: WS-R69 found that Razorpay refuses `updateSubscriptionQuantity` outright on a UPI Autopay or Emandate subscription, a real gap in the Suite lane's `updateOrgSeats`. Verified the supported path against Razorpay's own documents (`docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md` §29): `razorpay.com/docs/api/payments/subscriptions/update-subscription/`, fetched 2026-09-05, quoted verbatim, "the advised approach is to cancel and create a new Subscription if changes are needed" — and confirmed, by search, that no distinct "upgrade this mandate to a card" endpoint exists (`rejected.md#ws-r73-no-distinct-upi-to-card-upgrade-endpoint`), so cancel-and-recreate through Checkout is the one real path, not two. New `getSubscription` on both provider twins (`api/_payments/providers/razorpay.js`'s real `GET /v1/subscriptions/:id`, `fake.js`'s in-memory twin defaulting an unset ref to `'card'` so every pre-existing eval keeps passing, plus a test-only `setFakeSubscriptionMethod` and a call counter on `updateSubscriptionQuantity` so a refusal's "the provider was never reached" claim is provable, not just "the database was not written"); `updateOrgSeats` calls it BEFORE the PATCH and refuses, named `org_seats_locked_by_mandate` with the path in `details`, before either the provider call or the local seat write (`decisions.md#ws-r73-provider-read-not-a-ledger-column-for-the-mandate-method` — a provider read was chosen over a new ledger column precisely because it needs no migration and asks the one authority that actually knows). `copy.ts` gained a new top-level `suiteSeatLock` section (both locales, appended after the last existing section, never inside `suite`): a disclosure shown before checkout on `SuiteCard.tsx` (above "Start Suite subscription") and mirrored on `site/suites.html`'s own Pricing section in both locales, plus a named error message shown instead of the generic reason-code text when the refusal actually happens. `evals/org-billing/run.mjs` grew 40 to 50 (§6: UPI refused, Emandate refused, card still succeeds, each with a negative control on the provider call counter and a positive control proving the counter itself works); `evals/payments/run.mjs` grew 98 to 104 (§16: `getSubscription`'s real request shape, the no-field-present case, a missing-credentials negative control, the fake twin's default and setter); `evals/suites-self-serve/run.mjs` grew 60 to 68 (§7: the disclosure text proven present on the real page in both locales — by a Devanagari-range check, not a script-order guess, after the first version of that check misclassified the Hindi sentence because it opens with the Latin word "UPI" — and `SuiteCard.tsx` proven to actually render it before checkout and to special-case the real refusal). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` on the untouched tree (a separate worktree pinned to 8b154f8, since work had already started before the baseline run): 20/21 clean, `layout readability` EADDRINUSE on port 8931 across two attempts (a sibling wave-thirteen worktree holding the port, ws-common.md's own named collision), everything else including the room leak/export/door batteries, accessibility, performance and security headers green. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls, unchanged. `node scripts/check-mirrors.mjs`: 10 markers, 0 disagree (no new numeric constant to mirror — this workstream's copy states a fact, not a figure). `npx tsc -b`: clean. **The final gate on the CHANGED tree, honestly**: the shared machine carried ten wave-thirteen sibling worktrees running their own full gates at once (`ps aux` at one point showed a dozen concurrent `esbuild` processes across `ws-r72`/`r74`/`r75`/`r77`/`r79`/`r80` and this tree), so `verify-release.mjs` never completed in one unbroken process — one run reached `typecheck` through `eval suite` (16 of 21 static gates, layout readability and performance budgets both genuinely `ok` once the ports freed, 217s and 59s) before the container killed it (exit 144) ahead of the remaining battery gates; two further attempts reproduced only the SAME named `EADDRINUSE` collision on 8931/8932 already seen on the untouched tree, never a real failure. The remaining five (`room leak battery` 201/201, `room export completeness` 45/45, `room door battery` 544/544, `accessibility` 0 findings across 16 pages, `security headers` 0 findings) were each run standalone and passed; `evals/org-billing/run.mjs` (50/50), `evals/payments/run.mjs` (104/104) and `evals/suites-self-serve/run.mjs` (68/68) were also each run standalone directly, not only inside the combined suite. Every one of the 21 named gates is therefore confirmed passing against this exact tree, though never inside a single uninterrupted `verify-release.mjs` process — worth a session with a quieter machine re-running it whole. No migration, no new env var, no network beyond Razorpay's/GitHub's own public documentation pages (WebFetch/WebSearch) plus 127.0.0.1 and npm. Not merged, not pushed. **2026-09-05/06, WS-R77 built (CI runs the whole gate), on worktree branch `ws-r77-ci-runs-the-whole-gate` over `8b154f8`, not merged, not pushed, no migration.** `.github/workflows/release-gate.yml` runs `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` — the whole 21-check gate, no `NEON_URL` set so always 21 — on every push to `main` or a `claude/**` branch, Node 22 x Node 24 matrix, real Chromium, no secret (a stub config plus a static self-check that greps the workflow file for any `secrets.<NAME>` reference and fails if it finds one). Before this, `build-apk.yml` and `deploy-web.yml` ran `tsc -b` and the eval suite only — the room leak/door/export batteries, accessibility, performance budgets, security headers and the layout gate's glyph pass had never run in GitHub Actions at all, the same `gates-that-live-nowhere` shape `context/rejected.md` already names twice, this time at the scale of the whole gate. **Two real findings, both from actually proving the workflow rather than just writing it.** First, the no-secrets assertion itself had a bug: an unescaped `.` in a `grep` pattern matches ANY character, so the self-referential check (deliberately split across two shell variables so its own source never spells the substring it searches for) matched its own declaration line and would have failed on every run, clean tree or not — caught immediately by testing it against a known-clean input before trusting a red result, fixed with `grep -F` (`rejected.md#ws-r77-unescaped-dot-in-self-referential-secrets-grep-matches-everything`). Second, and more consequential: the workstream's own law 4 (install the CSS's real first-choice Devanagari font, `Noto Sans Devanagari`, as a system font via `fc-cache`, rather than trust whatever a runner's default fonts happen to substitute — this repo's own dev sandboxes render Hindi through GNU FreeFont's generous Devanagari coverage, which the existing `layout-gate-glyph-probe-uniformity-half-2026-09-05` measurement already admits would hide a genuinely missing webfont) worked exactly as intended and, for the first time, ran this repo's Hindi corpus through the CSS's actual named font — which surfaced a real, pre-existing bug in the glyph detector itself: real Hindi "सभी" (`threads.all`) measured 9.6% width-diff against tofu with its corroborating uniformity check returning `null` (2 base consonants + 1 matra, one short of the 3 base letters the sub-check needs), and the results filter's `!== false` treated that indeterminate `null` the same as a confirmed `true`, flagging it. Fixed by requiring `uniform === true` (positive confirmation from both signals, not "one confirmed and the other not disconfirmed"), verified against the full 979-string Hindi corpus (220 Room + 759 studio) via a standalone script comparing the old and new filter side by side: exactly the one finding removed, zero side effects, `MIN_GLYPH_DIFF_PCT` untouched (`rejected.md#ws-r77-glyph-uniform-null-treated-as-not-disproven-instead-of-not-confirmed`, which also names the accepted, narrow coverage tradeoff: a genuinely-tofu 2-base-letter-plus-matra word would now also not be flagged). **What is proven, and how:** the untouched tree gated 21/21 clean (baseline, before any change); the workflow YAML parses, passes `check-workflows.mjs`'s own lint, and its no-secrets assertion is proven both ways (a clean run, and a positive control with an injected `${{ secrets.FAKE_TOKEN }}` line, both by hand); the font-install mechanism (`fc-cache` into a scratch `$HOME`) is proven under both Node 22 (system, v22.22.2) and Node 24 (the real `node` npm package's own bundled binary, v24.20.0, since this sandbox has no `/opt/node24`) with a `fc-list` readback; `write-config.mjs --stub` and `evals/echosim/build.mjs` are proven under both Node versions. **What is NOT proven the same way, and why:** a fully clean, contention-free full `verify-release.mjs` re-run after the fix, on this exact worktree — every attempt (four, across both Node versions) collided with 8-10+ sibling wave-thirteen worktrees running their own full gates concurrently on this same 4-core sandbox (load average 18-21 throughout, confirmed by `ps` listing repeated identical `evals/run.mjs`/`verify-release.mjs` processes from `ws-r71` through `ws-r80`), each hit landing on `EADDRINUSE` for ports 8931-8933 (`ws-common.md`'s own documented, expected collision) or the eval suite step alone running 2.5-3x its baseline 218ms — a symptom, not a cause. The correctness claim rests instead on the targeted, full-corpus standalone verification above, which is a stronger proof of the SPECIFIC mechanism than a full-gate rerun would add, but a full concurrent-free `verify-release.mjs` pass on the finished tree is still owed and is the first thing to run before this branch merges. No migration; no new env var; every new SQL statement: none.

**2026-09-06, WS-R77 addendum: one full post-fix `verify-release.mjs` run completed.** The Node 22 run started before the report above and finished after it (11m45s of CPU-starved wall clock under the same sibling contention). Result: **18 of 21 checks passed clean**, including `eval suite` (736s, vs 218s baseline — the contention, not a defect), `room leak battery`, `room door battery`, `security headers`. The 3 failures — `layout readability`, `performance budgets`, `accessibility` — were each `EADDRINUSE` on 127.0.0.1:8931/8932/8933 at server startup, before any measurement ran; `layout readability` in particular never reached the glyph check this workstream fixed, so this run neither confirms nor contradicts that fix on its own (the full-corpus standalone verification already in the report above is what proves it). No other finding of any kind. Consistent with, and does not change, the report already given.

**2026-09-05, WS-R80 built (the taste on the creator's page, no migration), on worktree branch `ws-r80-taste-on-the-creator-page` over 8b154f8, not merged, not pushed.** WS-R53 built the taste inside the Room's own app shell; WS-R66 built the server-rendered `/c/<slug>` a crawler and a search visitor actually read. A visitor from a search result still had to click through to `/r/<slug>` before asking anything. This workstream puts the three questions on the page they landed on, as a small progressively-enhanced island: new `public/creator-taste.js` (5722 bytes raw, 2128 minified, under WS-R46's own 6KB budget) enhances a real, already-working no-JS `<form>` `api/_creator-page.js` now renders (a plain GET to `/r/<slug>?via=search`, the Room's own taste screen for a signed-out visitor) into an inline fetch against the exact same `taste` op `api/room.js` already serves — no new server code, no new op, the same 3-a-day `room_taste` rate scope, the same disclosure on the first answer, the same per-Room `taste_enabled` switch (now added to `publicCreatorPageRoomBySlug`'s own SELECT so the widget is absent, not hidden, when a creator turns it off). The island's copy (`TASTE_COPY`, both locales) restates `src/room/copy.ts`'s own `taste` section rather than importing it — `api/` cannot import a `.ts` module, the identical boundary `src/studio/pulseApi.ts` already names in the other direction — proven byte-identical by `evals/creator-page/run.mjs`'s new §12/§13 (89/89 total now), which bundles the REAL `copy.ts` with esbuild (`evals/room-locale/run.mjs`'s own technique) and compares field by field with a negative control. One real bug caught before merge, worth the read for anyone building a second island: the first draft wrote `data-room`/`data-locale` on the wrapping `<section>` while the script read them off the `<form>` it actually queries, so the island silently never mounted — no console error, no CSP violation — caught only by `scripts/check-headers.mjs`'s own `checkExecuted` proof for `/c/:slug` (now asserting the island's own `data-enhanced="1"` marker), which no HTML-substring eval assertion would ever have caught (`rejected.md#ws-r80-data-attributes-on-the-wrapping-section-not-the-element-the-script-reads`). A second, spec-level catch: the no-JS form's `action` carries NO query string, because a GET form submission REPLACES `action`'s own query string with the serialized fields rather than appending to it — `via=search` rides as a hidden field instead, or it would silently vanish for exactly the no-JS visitor this fallback exists to serve (`decisions.md#ws-r80-via-search-survives-as-a-hidden-field-not-in-the-form-action`). `evals/room-taste/run.mjs` gained a new §6 (32/32 total): the island parses, is dependency-free, fits the size cap, sends exactly one fetch (`/api/room`) and exactly one op literal (`taste`, never a follower op, with two negative controls), and never assigns `.innerHTML`. Performance: `/c/<slug>` stays at 2.2KB JS against the 180KB budget, LCP 344ms against WS-R66's own 448ms baseline (ordinary run noise, not a claimed win) (`measurements.md#ws-r80-creator-page-performance-2026-09-05`). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: 21/21 on the untouched tree (one `layout readability` EADDRINUSE on a sibling worktree's port reproduced and cleared on rerun, environmental per this repo's own rule), 21/21 after this workstream's own changes (`security headers` initially failed on the `checkExecuted` bug above, fixed, then green). `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean. No migration; the one SQL change is `taste_enabled` added to `publicCreatorPageRoomBySlug`'s existing SELECT column list (same table, same predicate, one more column) — listed for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`. No new env var. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: nobody has loaded `/c/<slug>` in a real browser against a real deployment; no real taste turn has ever been asked from this page outside a fake `db`. Did not push; on branch `ws-r80-taste-on-the-creator-page`.

**2026-09-05, WS-R76 built (the self-check cron, migration 120), on worktree branch `ws-r76-self-check-cron` over 8b154f8, not merged, not pushed.** Both Vercel projects ran for a day with no `NEON_URL` and every database door failing before anyone noticed (`measurements.md#live-probe-wave-eleven-preview-2026-09-05`); the incident ledger (WS-R58) records a 5xx only after a real request hits a broken door. This workstream is the deployment checking itself every morning: `api/self-check.js` (thin door, cron-secret gated like every other sweep) over `api/_self-check.js` (pure), through `withSweepRun("self-check", ...)`, runs four checks — (a) env presence by NAME only against two lists mirroring `scripts/write-config.mjs`'s own `STRINGS`/required-keys arrays exactly (kept in sync by a static parse of that file's own source text in the eval, never an import — that file writes `api/_config.js` and calls `process.exit` at module scope); (b) `select 1` through the real `api/_db.js`, so a missing URL surfaces as the exact `neon_url_missing` string that file already throws; (c) a small, explicit `information_schema` read (batched: one query for every anchor table, one for every anchor column, never one round trip per family) proving twelve representative migration-family tables plus one column are present in the live catalog; (d) every OTHER cron's own staleness against `vercel.json`'s schedule, self-check's own row excluded by name. Each failing check becomes one `recordIncident` row (`api/_incidents.js`, unchanged shape) with `kind: "self_check"` (migration 120 widens `vy_incident_kind_check` from five values to six, the live constraint name already explicit from migration 109 so no read-back ambiguity the way migration 113's inline-CHECK case had) and `door` the check's own name — "env: NEON_URL missing" reaches the ops board's existing Incidents card unmodified, and WS-R62's operator push fires once for the new kind, both for free, since neither was built kind-specific (`decisions.md#ws-r76-self-check-reports-through-the-existing-incident-ledger-not-a-second-pipeline`). The ops board gains a dedicated Self-check line (`api/_ops.js`'s new `selfCheckOverview`, derived from the SAME already-fetched `sweeps` array plus one new TODAY-only incident read — never `incidentsOverview`'s own 7-day rolling window, so a fixed finding stops appearing the next morning rather than lingering for a week) showing last run, checks passed/failed, and the failing checks' own names; `src/studio/OpsBoard.tsx`'s new `SelfCheckCard`, `src/studio/opsApi.ts`'s new `OpsSelfCheck` type. `vercel.json` gets one new cron, `30 2 * * *` (deliberately off the top of the hour every sibling cron fires on) — since the brief's own filename (`api/self-check.js`, not `-sweep.js`) breaks both `api/_sweep-schedule.js`'s naming convention and its minute-must-be-"0" schedule parser, both got one small, named, backward-compatible widening (`decisions.md#ws-r76-self-check-cron-path-is-the-one-named-exception-to-the-sweep-naming-convention`). One real defect found and fixed building this, the highest-value kind: three of the migration-family anchor tables the check's first draft named literally (102, 116, 101's own column) each collided with an existing `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` static scanner that matches ANY `api/*.js` source text substring — comments and JS string literals included, not just live queries — the third time this exact repo-wide gotcha has bitten a workstream (`rejected.md#ws-r76-migration-family-anchors-cannot-name-a-boundary-table-even-in-a-comment`, citing both WS-R54's and WS-R70's own prior entries); fixed by dropping the three anchors (one recovered via a safe column check on migration 110) rather than fighting an established, unrelated discipline. `evals/self-check/run.mjs` (new, 50/50, registered in `evals/run.mjs`): static parity against `scripts/write-config.mjs`'s real source, `checkDatabase`'s three-way split (`ok` / `neon_url_missing` / a generic content-free `select_1_failed`, never the raw driver message), a static leak scan of the real source with two NEGATIVE CONTROLS (a fixture reporting an env value's length or a prefix of one both fail it), a missing migration table AND a missing column each reported by their own name, a down database skipping (c)/(d) entirely rather than cascading false findings, and `recordSelfCheckIncidents`'s one-row-per-failing-check shape including a NEGATIVE CONTROL (a healthy result writes zero rows). `evals/ops/run.mjs` gained nine new assertions (a seeded self-check sweep row plus incidents feeding `overview.self_check` correctly, an honest never-ran empty state, no leakage from a different kind or a prior day) and `evals/incidents/run.mjs`'s closed-list assertion was widened from five to six. Gate results (run gate-by-gate rather than as one aggregate call, on a shared machine running several wave-thirteen siblings' own full gates concurrently for most of the session, load average 9-22 throughout): every gate passed, including `typecheck` (0 errors), the full `eval suite` (every registered suite, self-check's own 50/50 last), `room leak battery` (201/201, after the fix above — three transient failures during development, never shipped), `room export completeness` (45/45), `room door battery` (544/544 — `api/self-check.js` correctly excluded from the door list, it reads no request body and imports no door-decision module), `security headers`, and — once retried after several genuine `EADDRINUSE` collisions with sibling worktrees' own live gate processes on 8931/8932 (each confirmed by `lsof` naming the real PID and cwd, never killed) eased alongside falling load average — `performance budgets` (5 targets clean, `/studio` TBT 243ms against the 300ms budget, versus 468ms minutes earlier under heavier contention, confirming rather than contradicting this repo's own WS-R70 session-log precedent that TBT is load-sensitive on this machine), `accessibility` (0 critical/serious across 16 pages) and `layout readability` (1669 prose blocks, 979 Hindi strings). Full detail at `measurements.md#ws-r76-self-check-cron-gate-summary-2026-09-05`. Both are wired now, and a second flag action, "Sounds right anyway," is new. **The picker**: a new pure read, `api/_review-queue.js::readEligibleShowcaseCards`, reuses WS-R66's own eligibility predicate verbatim (`state = 'sounds_right' and kind <> 'follower_declined'`, one SELECT, never a JS filter) rather than composing a shared helper (`ws-r66-creator-page-predicate-restated-not-imported`'s own "restate, don't share" threshold, still inside budget at three copies); a new POST op `showcase_eligible` on `api/review-queue.js`; `ShowcaseCard.tsx` gets a "Pick from your reviews" control per slot that lists the results and calls the EXISTING `sourceCardId` write path (`decisions.md#ws-r72-showcase-picker-built`, supersedes the open item). **"Sounds right anyway"**: a new pure function, `dismissFlaggedReply`, DELETEs every `vy_room_reply_flag` row for one `(room, reply_sha256)`, owner-scoped through `vy_room`, no new column and no migration — the same DELETE-not-flip shape `unflagReply` already uses one file over, scaled from one follower's own row to every row the reply has (`decisions.md#ws-r72-flag-dismiss-is-a-creator-lane-delete-no-migration`); a new POST op `flag_dismiss`; `ReviewQueue.tsx` renders each flagged reply as its own card (the AI's own words, a real count, the real reason breakdown) with two buttons, "Never say this" (calling the now-wired `neverRuleFromFlag`, reusing `t.reviewQueue.buttonNever`/`noticeNever` verbatim) and "Sounds right anyway" (calling the new op). Both `showcasePicker` and `reviewQueueFlags` are NEW, closed `copy.ts` sections (both locales), never edits inside the existing `ShowcaseCopy`/`ReviewQueueCopy` blocks, per the wave's own merge-safety rule. **Two owner-bearer attack cases** were added to `evals/room-doors/run.mjs` directly on `api/_review-queue.js`'s two new functions, WITHOUT registering `api/review-queue.js` as a discovered door — `discoverDoors()` structurally excludes it (it imports `./_review-queue.js`, not one of `DOOR_MODULES`' closed set), and joining it would force real class-e coverage onto `generate`/`decide`/`dictate` too, three ops this workstream's own brief never named (`decisions.md#ws-r72-review-queue-js-kept-outside-the-door-battery`, a deliberate, logged scope boundary with its own reversal condition). **One real bug found building the door-battery case**: a new fixture branch for the picker's own SELECT was silently shadowed by an EARLIER, more generic WS-R66 branch sharing its matched substrings ("from vy_review_card" + "kind <> 'follower_declined'"), always returning an empty list for a query that should have matched real rows — fixed by narrowing the older branch to also require its own unique substring (`card_id = ($1)::uuid`), never present in the picker's own list read (`rejected.md#ws-r72-review-card-fixture-branch-shadowed-by-an-earlier-generic-match`). **The layout gate** renders the picker OPEN for the first time via a NEW "deploy-picker" step (`studio`/`studio-hi` targets) that publishes the fixture Room (`layoutFixture.tsx`'s new `showcase-picker` scenario — the base fixture's `room: null` had never let `ShowcaseCard` mount at all before this) and opens the picker with a REAL Playwright click on `[data-picker-open="1"]`, the SAME "never a fixture prop pre-opening it" law WS-R43 already established for the Room's own dialogs (`decisions.md#ws-r72-picker-opened-by-a-real-click-in-the-layout-gate`); since `.vy-room__showcase-slot` had literally no CSS rule before this workstream (never rendered by any gate), `roomStudio.css` also gained real styling for the slot and the new picker markup. Flagged-reply cards needed no new scenario at all: `flags`/`cards` were added directly to the BASE (non-scenario) `/api/review-queue` fixture route, so the `meet` step already exercises them in every existing pass, both locales. **The accessibility gate was deliberately NOT extended** with a picker screen: reaching a published Room in that gate would, for the first time, put `SuiteCard`/`PayoutsCard`/`CheckinsCard`/`HandoffCard` in front of axe too, four components this workstream never touched and has no budget to triage (`decisions.md#ws-r72-accessibility-gate-scope-flags-yes-picker-not-yet`, a named gap, not a silent one) — the flag actions ARE covered there already, for free, via the same base-fixture change. `evals/review-queue/run.mjs` gained three new sections (picker read, dismiss, and a NEW cross-owner negative control for `neverRuleFromFlaggedReply` that `evals/room-flags/run.mjs` did not yet carry): 129/129 (was 117). `evals/room-doors/run.mjs`: 549/549 (was 544). `evals/studio-shell/run.mjs` needed no change (its own concern, orphan-checking and tab headline models, is untouched by this workstream). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: on a heavily loaded shared machine (7+ concurrent sibling `verify-release.mjs` runs visible in `ps aux` throughout this session), the untouched tree itself measured 19 of 21 (`layout readability`/`performance budgets` both `EADDRINUSE`, ws-common.md's own named collision); after this workstream's changes, the SAME two checks, on the SAME two ports (8931/8932), are the ONLY failures on a full standalone rerun once contention eased — `eval suite`, `room leak battery`, `room export completeness`, `room door battery`, `accessibility` and `security headers` all pass clean. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean (1342 nodes, 1594 edges). **2026-09-05, WS-R79 built (language tagging for screen readers, no migration), on worktree branch `ws-r79-language-tagging` over `8b154f8`, not yet merged.** WS-R50's own accessibility gate proves axe finds no violation and the keyboard walk reaches every control, but neither ever asked whether a screen reader would PRONOUNCE the Room's Hindi correctly, and it does not: `document.documentElement.lang` names the follower's own chosen chrome locale, and four kinds of text are not guaranteed to be IN it. A single one-line detector per surface, appended to the end of `src/room/copy.ts` (`detectRoomTextLang`) and `src/studio/copy.ts` (`detectStudioTextLang`), tests a string's OWN characters for Devanagari rather than trusting any surrounding locale state — the load-bearing reason: `RoomApp.tsx`'s own `switchLocale` mints a fresh session and updates the document's `lang` the instant a follower switches language mid-conversation, but never refetches the disclosure text itself (`setRoomLocale`'s own return type carries no disclosure field), so the two can genuinely disagree (`decisions.md#ws-r79-tag-at-the-node-not-the-document`). Two new sibling files, `src/room/Localized.tsx` and `src/studio/Localized.tsx` (JSX kept out of `copy.ts` on purpose, matching `evals/room-locale/run.mjs`'s own standing comment that the file is "plain TS with no JSX" and gets bundled standalone), wired into: the Room's three disclosure-card renders (talk, taste, join screens) and the account page's own disclosure repeat, the "{name} AI" heading on the talk and taste screens, the taste screen's own creator-authored bio, and every `withName`-spliced heading (`join.title`, `taste.lede`, the account page's `forgetNote`) via a new `LocalizedName` helper that splits the template around `{name}` into real DOM siblings rather than one concatenated string; the studio's own `<h1>{replica.display_name}</h1>` (`StudioApp.tsx`); and `api/_creator-page.js`'s server-rendered `/c/<slug>` builder (a restated one-line detector, `api/` never importing from `src/`), tagging the creator's own name, bio, showcase question/answer pairs and the join link's own name portion, while the platform's OWN sentences stay untagged in the page's requested locale. Along the way, both `LanguageSwitch` (`RoomApp.tsx`) and `StudioLanguageSwitch` (`StudioShell.tsx`) buttons were found showing the OTHER language's own word with no `lang` of their own — fixed with `lang={l}`, the already-known locale, no detection needed (`decisions.md#ws-r79-language-switch-buttons-get-their-own-lang`; the group's own bilingual `aria-label` stays a named, un-fixed gap, no `.sr-only` utility existing in either stylesheet to build around it cheaply). `scripts/check-accessibility.mjs` gained `langTagAudit` (every Devanagari text node sits under a computed-`hi` ancestor; no element carrying its OWN `lang="hi"` attribute is ASCII-only, scoped to `document.body` so `<html lang="hi">` itself is never the target and to elements NOT tagged `hi-Latn`, a real, more specific BCP-47 tag `VoicePreviewPanel.tsx`'s own Hinglish sample correctly uses), its own always-on self-test (four planted fixtures, two per rule, mirroring `selfTest`'s own posture), and a new `creator-page` target that calls `buildCreatorPageHtml` directly with a deliberately mismatched Room (Hindi name/bio/showcase, page requested `?lang=en`) since `/c/<slug>` has no fixture route of its own. Two real bugs in the audit's own first draft were found and fixed by running it for real: a plain `SHOW_TEXT` tree walker read a JSON-LD `<script>` block's own payload as prose (`rejected.md#ws-r79-json-ld-script-text-is-not-prose`), and a `[lang^="hi-"]` prefix match flagged the correct `hi-Latn` Hinglish sample as ASCII-only-under-hi before the script subtag was actually read (`rejected.md#ws-r79-hi-latn-flagged-by-the-ascii-only-rule-before-the-script-subtag-was-read`, `decisions.md#ws-r79-lang-hi-latn-exempt-from-the-ascii-only-check`). Measured: before any render-site fix, 13 real findings across the room, room-hi, studio:shell and studio:shell-hi targets (all six language-switch instances, four studio ones); after every fix, `0 critical/serious across 17 page(s) ... 0 language-tag findings (221 Devanagari text node(s) checked, 37 own-attribute lang="hi" element(s) checked)`. A fired-and-reverted negative control against `api/_creator-page.js`'s own bio paragraph (removing `langSpan`, rerunning `--target creator-page`, restoring it) reproduced the exact planted mismatch and cleared on restore (`measurements.md#ws-r79-accessibility-lang-tag-coverage-2026-09-05`). New offline suite `evals/lang-tag/run.mjs` (31/31, registered in `evals/run.mjs`): every one of 218 Room and 750 studio REAL translated Hindi leaf strings detects as `hi` (shared, untranslated placeholders correctly skipped), eight named edge cases in both directions, and `buildCreatorPageHtml`'s real HTML output parsed with a regex for the exact tags this workstream's brief names. Baseline `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` on the untouched tree: 20 of 21 (the sole failure, `performance budgets`, reproduced clean on a standalone rerun — a shared-machine TBT blip on the untouched `/`, not this workstream's). `npx tsc -b` clean; `scripts/check-copy.mjs` clean (6 scopes, 21 negative controls, unchanged). `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 1341 nodes / 1593 edges. No migration, no new env var, no network beyond 127.0.0.1 and npm. Not proven: no real screen reader (TalkBack/VoiceOver/NVDA) was run against any of this; every proof is a computed-`lang`/DOM-shape assertion in Chromium. Not built: the language-switch group's own bilingual `aria-label`. Did not push; on branch `ws-r79-language-tagging`.

**2026-09-05, WS-R75 built (dormancy), on worktree branch `ws-r75-dormancy` over `8b154f8`, not merged, not pushed.** A follower's words have stayed in their private scope forever since day one; DPDP and plain decency say a relationship's data should not outlive the relationship by years. Migration 119: `vy_room.dormancy_days integer` (null means off, the default; CHECK `>= 180` when set) and `vy_room_follower.dormancy_notice_at timestamptz` (null until a notice went out) — no new person table, both columns ride an existing person-lane row. `api/_dormancy.js` (new) holds `dormancyNoticeDue` (one UPDATE...RETURNING: every follower whose `last_seen_at` is older than `dormancy_days - 30` and whose notice is null gets `dormancy_notice_at = now()`) and `dormancyForgetDue` (one SELECT: every follower whose notice is older than 30 days AND has not visited SINCE — `last_seen_at <= dormancy_notice_at`, read directly off the two timestamps rather than trusted from a cleared column, `decisions.md#ws-r75-dormancy-forget-predicate-is-self-contained`) and `dormancySweep` (the orchestrator, gated on `ROOM_DORMANCY==="1"`, off means neither statement runs). Forgetting goes through a genuinely new export, `roomForgetForFollower` in `api/_room-surface.js` — `roomForget` (the follower's own session-scoped op) is split into a thin session-resolving wrapper plus a shared `roomForgetCore`, so the sweep calls the EXACT same delete sequence, same child-before-parent ordering, same receipt, never a second delete path. `dormancySweep` runs from `api/renewals-sweep.js` alongside WS-R37's own `sweep()`, both summaries folded into the SAME `vy_sweep_run` row (`sweep='renewals'`) rather than a new cron entry (`decisions.md#ws-r75-dormancy-sweep-folded-into-renewals-cron`) — this workstream's own law 2's literal words, "the daily sweep gains two statements." The ops board's `dormancyThisWeek` (in `api/_ops.js`) reads a rolling 7-day SUM over that same `vy_sweep_run.counts` history, n>=5 floored, no new ledger table. Owner control: `api/_room-publish.js`'s `setRoomDormancyDays` / `api/room-publish.js`'s `set_dormancy_days` op, floor validated server-side (`ROOM_DORMANCY_DAYS_MIN=180`), wired into `evals/room-doors/run.mjs`'s OP_COVERAGE with a real owner-bearer case. Follower's own visit is defined by `joinRoom`'s existing `last_seen_at = now()` (unchanged) plus a new, purely defensive clear of `dormancy_notice_at` on the SAME ON CONFLICT UPDATE — the "one column" this workstream's own law 2 names; correctness never depends on it, only the account page's own sentence reading current quickly does. Delivery channels: in-app is the timestamp itself (no network); Telegram reuses `activeTelegramChannelFor`/`sendRoomCheckinMessage` verbatim, real and functional; web push is deliberately NOT attempted — **a real, previously undiscovered gap found building this workstream**: `public/room-sw.js`'s own push handler drops any payload whose `t` is not the literal string `"checkin"`, so WS-R37's own `t:"renewal"` push has been silently discarded on arrival since it shipped, unproven end to end, and this workstream declined to add a second silently-dead `t:"dormancy"` payload on top of it (`rejected.md#ws-r75-web-push-type-switch-drops-every-non-checkin-payload`); WhatsApp stays structurally present and inert, no dormancy-specific template is approved (`decisions.md#ws-r75-dormancy-whatsapp-channel-left-inert`). Front end: the follower's account page (`AccountPage.tsx`) renders one sentence ("Kept until {duration} after your last visit") only when the Room has a policy, both locales, a new `dormancyDurationLabel` helper rendering whole years/months/days rather than a raw day count; the creator's Room Studio gets a new card naming the 180-day floor with three presets plus a free-entry field. **A real defect caught before the first eval ran**: explaining the `dormancy_notice_at = null` line with a SQL comment that used JS backticks around identifier names closed the surrounding template literal early — the exact defect `rejected.md#ws-r37-sql-comment-backticks-terminate-the-template-literal-a-third-time` already named, a fourth time, caught immediately by `node --check` (`rejected.md#ws-r75-sql-comment-backticks-terminate-the-template-literal-a-fourth-time`). New suite `evals/room-dormancy/run.mjs` (37/37): the real `dormancyNoticeDue`/`dormancyForgetDue`/`dormancySweep` and the real `roomForgetForFollower` over a fake db, two negative controls (a forget with no prior notice is structurally unreachable; a follower who visited after their notice is never forgotten even with the notice column left uncleared). `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` gained layer 11 (a forget in one Room, driven by that Room's own overdue follower, never touches another Room's own follower row/name/receipt; struck-predicate negative control DOES leak across Rooms) — 207/207, 535 boundary checks (+5), and `api/_dormancy.js` admitted to the leak battery's `ALLOWED` set (its two statements read several of a follower's own columns, not an aggregate, but act only on that same follower's own scope, never creator-facing). `evals/room-export/run.mjs` gained one assertion (46/46): `dormancy_notice_at` rides `vy_room_follower`'s row through the EXISTING generic `select *` `roomScopedTables()` loop already runs, no code change needed to carry it. `evals/room-doors/run.mjs` 551/551 (+5, `set_dormancy_days`'s own owner-bearer case). `evals/ops/run.mjs` 125/125 (+1, honest empty state). `node evals/run.mjs`: exit 0, every suite including `room-dormancy` (registered last). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **21/21** on this workstream's own tree after the typecheck fixes above (three real TS errors: `settings` narrowing in `AccountPage.tsx`, a missing `dormancy_days` field in `layoutFixture.tsx`'s `RoomSettings` fixture, and `RoomStudio.tsx`'s `busy` state union missing `"dormancy"`, all fixed). The UNTOUCHED tree (a separate `git worktree` at `8b154f8`) reproduced **20/21 across four attempts** in this heavily concurrent wave-thirteen environment, the ONE failure a different `EADDRINUSE` port each time (8931, then 8932, then 8933) — a sibling worktree's own gate holding the port, `ws-common.md`'s own named collision, never the same check twice, the signature of contention rather than a defect; every one of typecheck/eval-suite/leak-battery/export-completeness/door-battery/security-headers passed on every baseline attempt. `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: clean, 6 scopes, 21 negative controls. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 1341 nodes / 1593 edges. **New env var** `ROOM_DORMANCY` (`docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md` §29), off by default, gates the sweep step only — the columns and the owner control work regardless. NOT PROVEN, stated plainly: no statement this workstream wrote has ever run against a live Postgres (no `NEON_URL` in this environment; every new SQL statement is listed verbatim in the workstream's own final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`); no real `dormancy_days`/`dormancy_notice_at` value exists anywhere outside a fake `db`; no real Telegram notice has ever been sent by this mechanism; no human has ever seen the account-page sentence or the studio's dormancy card render in a browser; `scripts/relcheck.mjs` did not run (no `NEON_URL`) — not needed here regardless, since no new table was created. Migration 119 is this workstream's only number, per the wave-thirteen brief; did not push.

**2026-09-05, WS-R71 built (the studio's last panels in Hindi, tier 2 wave three), on worktree branch `ws-r71-studio-hindi-last-panels` over `8b154f8`, not merged, not pushed.** Wave twelve's own closing line named the gap: "the nineteen studio panels still in English." This workstream reads every remaining `TIER_2_ALLOWLIST` entry in full before choosing what to move — not just its one-line reason — and converts six: `ActivityPanel.tsx`, `ChannelsStudio.tsx`, `TeacherSheetStudio.tsx`, `VoicePreviewLab.tsx`, `VoicePreviewPanel.tsx`, `VoiceExperimentPanel.tsx` (2,430 lines of component source, `decisions.md#ws-r71-tier-2-second-wave-converted`). `src/studio/copy.ts` gains six new sections, 395 new leaf strings per locale (759 to 1,154; `measurements.md#ws-r71-studio-hindi-tier-2-second-wave-2026-09-05`), added as new interface/object blocks after the existing `creatorExport`/`showcase` ones rather than edited into them, this wave's own append-only law for shared files. All four are left whole and unconverted, each with a strengthened, file-specific allowlist reason naming exactly what its ceremony is (`decisions.md#ws-r71-consent-ceremony-files-found-and-not-converted`, citing `rejected.md#ws-r61-partial-modelconsentgate-translation-considered-and-rejected`'s own "do not split the words from the screen" rule). `TIER_2_ALLOWLIST` goes from 20 entries to 16 (six converted out, four newly strengthened in place, four "not reached this session, time-boxed" — `ContextLockerPanel.tsx`, `EnrollmentWorkspace.tsx`, `MirrorCallStudio.tsx`, `VoiceEnrollmentLab.tsx` — plus the unchanged structural three). Eight sibling evals were found (by filename grep, then a second distinctive-sentence grep across `evals/`/`scripts/`, the two-pass heuristic `rejected.md#ws-r61-assumed-studio-locale-and-check-copy-were-sufficient-gates-for-a-tier-2-move` recommends) to also read one of the six converted files' raw source and were updated to read `component (+ copy.ts)` together: `evals/voicepanel.mjs`, `evals/replicaactivity.mjs`, `evals/voice-preference/run.mjs`, `evals/open-voice/run.mjs`, `evals/studio-self-test-ui/run.mjs`, `evals/voice-delivery-policy/run.mjs`, `evals/voice-delivery-holdout/run.mjs`, `evals/voice-preview-ui.mjs`. One real defect surfaced and was fixed while doing that: concatenating the WHOLE of `copy.ts` (rather than just the relevant section) into `voice-preview-ui.mjs`'s own check false-tripped its "no unmeasured quality claim" finding on a DIFFERENT section's correct, negated sentence ("...not an automatic winner") — fixed by slicing just the one relevant section instead (`rejected.md#ws-r71-concatenating-the-whole-copy-ts-leaked-a-sibling-sections-wording-into-an-unrelated-check`). `node evals/studio-locale/run.mjs`: 56/56 (0 literal English JSX text nodes across all six new Tier 1 files, all 1,154 real Hindi strings pass the real copy gate). `node scripts/check-copy.mjs`: 6 scopes clean, 21 negative controls. `npx tsc -b --noEmit`: clean, first attempt. `node evals/run.mjs` (the full suite, inside `verify-release.mjs`): PASSED, 266,869ms. **One real, reproducible, non-environmental gate finding this workstream did NOT resolve:** `performance budgets` fails on `/studio`'s JS transfer, 183.2KB against a 180KB budget — confirmed real (not machine-load noise) by three separate runs all reporting the identical 183.2KB figure while TBT swung from 508ms (a loaded machine, 8+ concurrent sibling gates) down to 125-177ms (isolated), which the byte metric never moved with. `copy.ts` grew 168,806 to 256,049 raw bytes this session; its post-gzip contribution is what crossed the ceiling. This is an architecture question (code-splitting `copy.ts` by locale or by lazy panel boundary, neither of which exists today — every consumer imports the whole table eagerly) or a budget-recalibration question (five successive translation workstreams have now grown this one file), not a same-session fix this workstream attempted: trimming translated prose to claw back exactly 3.2KB would be gaming the metric at the cost of the wording quality the brief asked for, the opposite trade this repo's own law forbids. Flagged for the owner/main loop rather than patched around. `layout readability` and `accessibility` both confirmed clean: `accessibility` passed inside the one full run that got that far (39,452ms), mounting every studio panel in both locales and finding zero critical/serious axe findings; `layout readability` needed a standalone re-run after a live sibling worktree (`ws-r75`) held port 8931 for over ten minutes (`ws-common.md`'s own documented one-gate-per-machine collision) and came back clean once the port freed: 1,663 prose blocks judged across every `studio`/`studio-hi`/`room`/`room-hi` target at 390/834/1355px, 1,374 Hindi strings glyph-checked (1,357 width-tested), zero findings. **Gate summary: 20 of 21 checks confirmed clean on this tree** (typecheck, prompt budget, workflow lint, motion lint, board legibility, chrome copy, mirrored constants, enrollment sample rate, enrollment bandwidth, engine bundle fresh, stuck-turn endpoint, one voice, web build, layout readability, eval suite, room leak battery, room export completeness, room door battery, accessibility, security headers); performance budgets is the one real, unresolved failure, for the reason above. No migration; no new env var. Did not push; on branch `ws-r71-studio-hindi-last-panels`.

**2026-09-05, WS-R88 built (the operator's morning digest, migration 125), on worktree branch `ws-r88-operator-morning-digest` over `6deaf1e`, not merged, not pushed.** WS-R62 gave an operator a push subscription and WS-R76 taught the deployment to check on itself every morning, but neither answers the ops board's own everyday questions unless someone opens it. This workstream is the one push a day that does: `api/_operator-digest.js` (new) holds `digestCounts` (a PURE reduction of `api/_ops.js#opsOverview`'s own already-fetched shape down to rooms published, followers joined this week, messages, revenue, the self-check's own checked/failed and whether it has run at all, and today's incident count/new-kind count — never a new query against a person table, per law 2; reads only `.published`/`.joined_last_7d`/`.messages_last_24h`/`.revenue_this_month_inr` off each Room, never `.slug`/`.display_name`, proven by a static-scan negative control in the new eval), `operatorDigestPayload` (the WS-R22 "parameter list is the enforcement" shape, body under 200 characters, a second static-scan negative control proving its own source names none of this repo's follower/Room-content columns), `sendOperatorDigest` (the sweep's own claim against migration 125's `vy_operator_digest(digest_id, day, sent_at, counts)`, `day` UNIQUE the whole idempotency mechanism — one digest per day, never per subscription, `vy_creator_weekly_push`'s own `(room_id, week_start)` pattern restated platform-wide) and `sendTestOperatorDigest` (law 4: sends to the CALLING operator's own active subscriptions only, title prefixed "TEST", writes NO ledger row, refuses a bearer not on `OPS_OWNER_USER_IDS` even called directly — `isOpsOwnerLocal`'s own explicit check, since there is no SQL WRITE on this read-and-send path for a WHERE clause to decide on the way `subscribeOperatorPush`'s own INSERT does). **The n>=5 follower floor lives inside `digestCounts`, not the payload builder**: `followers_joined_below_floor` is a boolean the payload builder branches on, and its floored branch never reads the raw sub-5 count at all — structurally unreachable, not merely unused (`decisions.md#ws-r88-follower-floor-applied-inside-digestCounts-not-the-payload-builder`). **This file never imports `api/_ops.js` back** — the ONE direction that IS safe is `api/_ops.js` importing this file's own `lastOperatorDigest` for the board's "Last digest" line; every `_ops.js` function the WRITE path needs is either an injected `deps` function (`opsOverviewFn`/`operatorSubscriptionsFor`/`revokeOperatorSubscription`/`sendPush`, `api/_incidents.js#notifyNewIncidentKinds`'s own shape) or a local restatement (`opsOwnerIdsLocal`, `isOpsOwnerLocal`, `operatorDigestConfig` — the third instance of the pattern `api/_incidents.js`/`api/_creator-push.js` already established), avoiding the exact `_ops.js -> _incidents.js -> _ops.js` cycle shape `rejected.md#ws-r58-incidents-importing-opsownerids-from-ops-js-makes-a-cycle` already names (`decisions.md#ws-r88-operator-digest-never-imports-ops-js`). The cron: `api/operator-digest-sweep.js` (new), 03:15 UTC daily in `vercel.json` (after `self-check` at 02:30, so the digest carries that morning's own verdict), `CRON_SECRET`-gated, through `withSweepRun`, wiring both files' real functions together — the ONE place both are imported. `api/ops.js` gains a third owner-bearer POST op, `send_test_digest`, cased in `OP_COVERAGE["ops.js"]` and exercised in a new `evals/room-doors/run.mjs` §17d (class e: calling `sendTestOperatorDigest` directly with a bearer NOT on `OPS_OWNER_USER_IDS`, who nonetheless holds a real subscription row of their own, pushes to NOBODY). `src/studio/opsApi.ts` gains `OpsDigest`/`sendTestOpsDigest`; `OpsBoard.tsx` gains a `DigestCard` ("Last digest: {time}", a "Send a test digest now" button) — **copy stays inline English, not `copy.ts`/`hiCopy.ts`**, restating `decisions.md#ws-r62-ops-board-push-copy-stays-english-inline` for a second card on the same `TIER_2_ALLOWLIST` page (`decisions.md#ws-r88-ops-board-digest-copy-stays-english-inline`) — a deliberate departure from the brief's own file list, not an oversight. New suite `evals/operator-digest/run.mjs`: 49/49 (config, the floor, the payload's two static-scan negative controls plus NEGATIVE CONTROL (b) — a sub-5 count never appears as an exact number, the ledger claim's own idempotency across two sweep ticks and two different days, a 404 revoking only the dead subscription, a missing `opsOverviewFn` throwing loudly rather than silently no-op-ing, the test op writing no ledger row). `evals/ops/run.mjs`: 137/137 (135 before, two new `overview.digest` assertions — an honest null with nothing ever sent, and the MOST RECENT day's row surfacing once two are seeded). `evals/room-doors/run.mjs`: 569/569 (566 before, three new assertions in §17d). `node --check` clean on every new/touched `.js` file; `npx tsc --noEmit -p .` clean, no output. `node scripts/verify-release.mjs`: **20 of 21** on both a standalone baseline (a separate `git worktree` at `6deaf1e`, before any change) and this workstream's own full tree — the SAME single failure both times, `EADDRINUSE` on a different port each run (8931 on the baseline, 8932 on this tree), `ws-common.md`'s own documented one-gate-per-machine collision under this wave's heavy concurrent load (ten-plus sibling `verify-release.mjs` runs visible in `ps aux` throughout this session); `eval suite` (which bundles and runs `operator-digest`/`ops`/`room-doors` among every other suite) passed clean on both runs. `node scripts/context.mjs --check`: clean, 1408 nodes / 1650 edges (append-only — the first attempt at appending via a JSON re-dump reformatted the WHOLE file's escaping, `—` to a literal em dash, a 154-line diff for a 4-node append; reverted and redone as a surgical text insertion before the previous line's own closing bracket, landing a 39-line pure-append diff instead). No `rejected.md` entry: nothing tried this session broke on an actual run (a params-destructuring mismatch in the new eval's own fake db was caught by re-reading the SQL before the eval was ever run, not by a failed run). **2026-09-05, WS-R87 built (Handoff v1 on the relational kernel, no migration), on worktree branch `ws-r87-handoff-on-the-relational-kernel` over `6deaf1e`, not merged, not pushed.** The plan's Phase 3 names the GroupAI kernel (`/home/user/Vyakti-GroupAI`, `packages/relational-core`) as the engine for Suites, Handoff and Bridge, "proven on 960 synthetic worlds with zero leaks." This workstream brings the evaluator Handoff v0 (WS-R20, migration 083) already needed a predicate for, not the whole package: new `api/_relational-core.js`, a dependency-free port (no imports at all) of the sibling's `privacy.ts` (read at commit `9cdc1dc`) — a closed disclosure-act list (`influence`/`gist`/`paraphrase`/`verbatim`, byte-identical to the sibling's own enum), deny-always-wins checked first and unconditionally, a grant `{from, to, act, scope, policy_version, expires_at}` bound to an exact policy version, expiry as an EXCLUSIVE boundary (ported directly from the sibling's own test naming that), and a named refusal rather than a bare boolean. **Migration 124 was NOT needed and was not created**: `vy_room_handoff.policy_version` already exists (migration 083's own column, default 1), so the brief's own "prefer none" held. `api/_handoff.js` wires it into `sendHandoffRequest` (before the INSERT, follower to Room, `verbatim`, under `HANDOFF_POLICY_VERSION` — no new SQL statement either way, so the INSERT is byte-identical flag on or off, proven by diffing the executed SQL text in the eval) and `answerHandoff` (the creator's reply "the other way," Room to follower, under the policy version ALREADY STORED on the row — one new pre-read SELECT, only when the flag is on) behind `ROOM_HANDOFF_KERNEL` (env var, off by default, `docs/gurukul/ENV-MANIFEST.md` §31). In v0 every grant evaluated is SELF-ISSUED (the follower's or creator's own explicit submission satisfies its own request, `decisions.md#ws-r87-handoff-v0-grant-is-self-issued`) — the one reachable refusal path is `deps.handoffDenies`, a seam for a future creator-side deny list that no production code populates yet, proven real (not decorative) by the eval populating it directly and getting `handoff_kernel_denied` back, on both send and answer. New `evals/relational-core/run.mjs` (25/25, registered): every vector ported by hand from the sibling's `privacy.test.ts`/`privacy-matrix.test.ts`, cited by file and line range, including the two negative controls the brief names directly ("a grant whose scope is another Room is refused," "a deny beats a grant") and an EXHAUSTIVE 256-case independent-oracle cross-check standing in for the sibling's own 500-case `fast-check` random sweep (`decisions.md#ws-r87-oracle-cross-check-is-exhaustive-not-fast-check-sampled` — this repo has no `fast-check` dependency and the port introduces none). `evals/handoff/run.mjs` extended 30 to 40: flag-off/flag-on SQL diff, and the deny-list wiring proof on both send and answer. `evals/room-leak/run.mjs` layer 6 (consented-only) now runs its whole world check TWICE inside one execution, flag unset and flag `"1"`, zero leaks both times (the brief's own law 4, verbatim) — whole suite 223/223, 336,323 retrieval row-scenario checks, 546 boundary checks. One real defect found building this: the header comment explaining why the sibling's full policy model was left behind named `vy_room_handoff` literally, tripping `evals/room-leak/run.mjs`'s repo-wide static scan a FOURTH time (after WS-R54/WS-R70/WS-R76's own prior hits of the identical trap, this time against a file that issues no SQL at all) — fixed by paraphrasing rather than fighting the established discipline (`rejected.md#ws-r87-explaining-vy-room-handoff-in-a-comment-tripped-the-leak-batterys-static-scan-a-fourth-time`). `docs/gurukul/HANDOFF-KERNEL.md` (new): what was ported line by line against the sibling's own file, what stayed behind and why, how the flag wires in, and what Bridge would need next (a real grants table, a real deny-list reader, possibly non-verbatim acts, and — only once a second real caller needs it — more of the sibling's own multi-owner policy shape). `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` on the untouched tree: **21/21** (`measurements.md#ws-r87-...` not separately logged — the baseline matched AGENTS.md's own stated count exactly, no environmental noise on this run). After every change in this report: `node evals/relational-core/run.mjs` 25/25, `node evals/handoff/run.mjs` 40/40, `node evals/room-leak/run.mjs` 223/223, `node evals/run.mjs` (all 28 registered suites) exit 0 with zero failures, `node scripts/check-copy.mjs` 6 scopes clean/21 negative controls unchanged, `npx tsc -b` clean, `node scripts/check-headers.mjs` standalone clean (0 findings, 7 targets). The full `node scripts/verify-release.mjs` on the changed tree hit the SAME named `EADDRINUSE` collision on 8931/8932 this repo's own `ws-common.md` documents (two sibling worktrees' gate processes, PIDs confirmed by `lsof`, holding those ports for the whole session) — `layout readability` and `performance budgets` did not get a clean standalone rerun in this session's own time budget; `security headers` (which also failed once on the same shared-machine contention, port 8934) was confirmed clean standalone once that port freed. No migration: `vy_room_handoff.policy_version` already existed. No new SQL against a live database (no `NEON_URL`) — the one new statement (`answerHandoff`'s pre-read SELECT, gated behind the flag) is listed verbatim in this workstream's own final report for the main loop to `EXPLAIN`. Not proven: no `ROOM_HANDOFF_KERNEL=1` request has ever reached a live deployment; the sibling repo's own kernel was read, never executed, by this workstream.
